From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia

2023–2024 concert tour by Taylor Swift

"The Eras" redirects here. For other uses, see [Eras (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eras_(disambiguation) "Eras (disambiguation)"). This article is about the concert tour. For the concert film, see [*Taylor Swift: The Eras Tour*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift:_The_Eras_Tour "Taylor Swift: The Eras Tour").

The Eras Tour

**Tour by [Taylor Swift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift "Taylor Swift")**

**The Eras Tour** was the sixth [concert tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concert_tour "Concert tour") by the American singer-songwriter [Taylor Swift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift "Taylor Swift"). It began in [Glendale, Arizona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glendale,_Arizona "Glendale, Arizona"), United States, on March 17, 2023, and concluded in [Vancouver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vancouver "Vancouver"), British Columbia, Canada, on December 8, 2024. Spanning 149 shows in 51 cities across 21 countries and five continents, the Eras Tour had [a large cultural and socioeconomic impact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impact_of_the_Eras_Tour "Impact of the Eras Tour"). It became the [highest-grossing tour of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_concert_tours "List of highest-grossing concert tours") and the first to earn over $1 billion and $2 billion in revenue.

Announced after the release of her tenth studio album *[Midnights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midnights "Midnights")* (2022), Swift designed the tour as a retrospective tribute to [all of her albums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift_albums_discography "Taylor Swift albums discography") and their corresponding musical "eras". Running over 3.5 hours, the [set list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Set_list "Set list") consisted of over 40 songs grouped into 10 [acts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Act_(drama) "Act (drama)") that portrayed each album's mood and aesthetic. The show was revamped in May 2024 to incorporate her eleventh studio album, *[The Tortured Poets Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Tortured_Poets_Department "The Tortured Poets Department")* (2024). Critics praised the Eras Tour for its concept, production, and immersive ambience, as well as Swift's vocals, stage presence, and versatile showmanship.

The tour recorded unprecedented public demand, ticket sales and attendances, bolstering economies, businesses, and tourism worldwide, dominating [social media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_media "Social media") and [news cycles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/24-hour_news_cycle "24-hour news cycle"), and garnering tributes from governments and organizations. This also gave rise to multifarious issues: ticketing crashes that inspired a string of anti-[scalping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scalping_(trading) "Scalping (trading)") laws and price regulation policies; [scrutiny of Ticketmaster for monopoly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift–Ticketmaster_controversy "Taylor Swift–Ticketmaster controversy") by US authorities; [diplomatic tensions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_incident "International incident") in [Southeast Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Asia "Southeast Asia") due to [Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore "Singapore")'s exclusivity grant; poor venue management in [Rio de Janeiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_de_Janeiro "Rio de Janeiro") resulting in [a death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Ana_Clara_Benevides "Death of Ana Clara Benevides"); [a failed ISIS plot to attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Vienna_terrorism_plot "2024 Vienna terrorism plot") the tour in [Vienna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vienna "Vienna"); and [a political scandal in the UK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Labour_Party_freebies_controversy "2024 Labour Party freebies controversy").

Swift disclosed and released various works throughout the tour: the [re-recorded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Re-recorded "Re-recorded") albums *[Speak Now (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speak_Now_(Taylor's_Version) "Speak Now (Taylor's Version)")* and *[1989 (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_(Taylor's_Version) "1989 (Taylor's Version)")* in 2023; editions of *Midnights* and *The Tortured Poets Department*; the music videos of "[Karma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karma_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Karma (Taylor Swift song)")", "[I Can See You](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Can_See_You_(song) "I Can See You (song)")", and "[I Can Do It with a Broken Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Can_Do_It_with_a_Broken_Heart "I Can Do It with a Broken Heart")"; and "[Cruel Summer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruel_Summer_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Cruel Summer (Taylor Swift song)")" as a [single](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Single_(music) "Single (music)"). [An accompanying concert film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift:_The_Eras_Tour "Taylor Swift: The Eras Tour"), documenting the [Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles "Los Angeles") shows, was released to theaters worldwide on October 13, 2023, in an uncommon [distribution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_distribution "Film distribution") deal circumventing [major film studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_film_studios "Major film studios"). Met with critical acclaim, the film became [the highest-grossing concert film in history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concert_film#Highest-grossing_concert_films "Concert film"). A [photo book](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Photo_book "Photo book") of the tour, *[The Eras Tour Book](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Eras_Tour_Book "The Eras Tour Book")*, was released on November 29, 2024. A [behind-the-scenes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Behind-the-scenes "Behind-the-scenes") series, [*The End of an Era*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift:_The_End_of_an_Era "Taylor Swift: The End of an Era"), and a second concert film based on the Vancouver shows, *The Final Show*, were released on December 12, 2025, on [Disney+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney+ "Disney+"). The tour's accolades include an [iHeartRadio Music Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IHeartRadio_Music_Award "IHeartRadio Music Award") for Tour of the Century and six *[Guinness World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_World_Records "Guinness World Records")*. Swift's life as an entertainer during the tour inspired her twelfth studio album, *[The Life of a Showgirl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Life_of_a_Showgirl "The Life of a Showgirl")* (2025).

## Background

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=1 "Edit section: Background")]

[![Taylor Swift wearing a black dress singing on a mic](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/Taylor_Swift_-_Reputation_Tour_Seattle_-_King_of_My_Heart_1.jpg/250px-Taylor_Swift_-_Reputation_Tour_Seattle_-_King_of_My_Heart_1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_-_Reputation_Tour_Seattle_-_King_of_My_Heart_1.jpg)

[Taylor Swift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift "Taylor Swift") on the [Reputation Stadium Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reputation_Stadium_Tour "Reputation Stadium Tour") (2018), the highest-grossing North American concert tour before the Eras Tour

[Taylor Swift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift "Taylor Swift")'s last concert tour, the [Reputation Stadium Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reputation_Stadium_Tour "Reputation Stadium Tour") (2018), broke the record for the highest-grossing US tour in history.[[2]](#cite_note-4) She cancelled a planned concert tour in support of her seventh studio album [*Lover*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lover_(album) "Lover (album)") (2019), due to the outbreak of the [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic "COVID-19 pandemic") in 2020,[[3]](#cite_note-5) and subsequently released three new studio albums: [*Folklore*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore_(Taylor_Swift_album) "Folklore (Taylor Swift album)") (2020), *[Evermore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evermore "Evermore")* (2020), and *[Midnights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midnights "Midnights")* (2022). As part of her [re-recording](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Re-recording_(music) "Re-recording (music)") project to claim ownership over the [masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mastering_(audio) "Mastering (audio)") of her past albums, she released two re-recorded albums, *[Fearless (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fearless_(Taylor's_Version) "Fearless (Taylor's Version)")* and *[Red (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_(Taylor's_Version) "Red (Taylor's Version)")*, in 2021.[[4]](#cite_note-Willman-2022b-6)

During promotion for *Midnights*, on November 1, 2022, she announced the Eras Tour, which she described as "a journey through the musical eras of [her] career", on *[Good Morning America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Morning_America "Good Morning America")* and through her social media accounts.[[5]](#cite_note-Jones-2022-7)[[6]](#cite_note-8) Messina Touring Group, an [Anschutz Entertainment Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anschutz_Entertainment_Group "Anschutz Entertainment Group") (AEG) partner, was the tour's promoter.[[7]](#cite_note-Pollstar-2023c-9) She first announced 27 US shows from March to August 2023, beginning in [Glendale, Arizona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glendale,_Arizona "Glendale, Arizona"), and ending in [Inglewood, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inglewood,_California "Inglewood, California"). The [opening acts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opening_act "Opening act") were [Paramore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramore "Paramore"), [Haim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haim_(band) "Haim (band)"), [Phoebe Bridgers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoebe_Bridgers "Phoebe Bridgers"), [Beabadoobee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beabadoobee "Beabadoobee"), [Girl in Red](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Girl_in_Red "Girl in Red"), [Muna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muna_(band) "Muna (band)"), [Gayle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gayle_(singer) "Gayle (singer)"), [Gracie Abrams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gracie_Abrams "Gracie Abrams"), and Owenn, each two of whom shared a tour date.[[4]](#cite_note-Willman-2022b-6) Popular demand led Swift to announce eight additional US shows on November 4,[[8]](#cite_note-10) and 17 the following week.[[9]](#cite_note-Willman-2022a-11) A second US leg was announced on August 4, 2023, with Abrams as the opener.[[10]](#cite_note-Kreps-2023-12) *[Billboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_(magazine) "Billboard (magazine)")* thought that the huge demand contributed to "the most chaos-inducing tour announcement of the decade".[[11]](#cite_note-13)

On June 2, 2023, Swift announced the Latin American shows of the Eras Tour, with [Sabrina Carpenter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabrina_Carpenter "Sabrina Carpenter") as the opening act.[[12]](#cite_note-Garcia-2023-14) A third show in [Buenos Aires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buenos_Aires "Buenos Aires") was announced on June 6, 2 hours after the public sale commenced, due to high demand.[[13]](#cite_note-15) [Louta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louta_(musician) "Louta (musician)") was announced as an additional opening act.[[14]](#cite_note-16) Additional shows in [Mexico City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico_City "Mexico City"), [Rio de Janeiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_de_Janeiro "Rio de Janeiro"), and [São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/São_Paulo "São Paulo") were announced on June 12.[[15]](#cite_note-17) Shows in Asia, Australia, and Europe were announced on June 20.[[16]](#cite_note-Dailey-2023-18) From June to November 2023, new shows were added to the tour. In Asia-Pacific, six extra shows were added to [Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore "Singapore"),[[17]](#cite_note-19) and two to Australia.[[18]](#cite_note-Williams-2023-20) In Europe, eight shows were added initially,[[19]](#cite_note-Willman-2023a-21) followed by fourteen more,[[20]](#cite_note-Willman-2023b-22) then two in [London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London "London");[[21]](#cite_note-23) [Paramore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramore "Paramore") was announced as the leg's opening act.[[20]](#cite_note-Willman-2023b-22) Nine shows were also announced in Canada ([Toronto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toronto "Toronto") and [Vancouver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vancouver "Vancouver")), with Abrams as the opening act.[[22]](#cite_note-24)[[23]](#cite_note-25) In February 2024, a second show was added in [Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madrid "Madrid") after the venue's football club, [Real Madrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_Madrid "Real Madrid"), asked [La Liga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Liga "La Liga") to reschedule their match to cover the "extraordinary demand".[[24]](#cite_note-26)

Several politicians and government officials demanded the tour be brought to their country or city.[[25]](#cite_note-27) Places such as Canada and Chile were expected to be part of the tour but were absent in Swift's announcement on June 20, drawing dismay and demands from fans and officials.[[26]](#cite_note-28)[[27]](#cite_note-29) Across [Southeast Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Asia "Southeast Asia"), fans decried the lack of shows in countries like the Philippines, Malaysia, Indonesia, Thailand and [Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong "Hong Kong") and demanded the tour be brought to there. In particular, her return to the Philippines had been highly anticipated.[[28]](#cite_note-30) Some journalists and fans opined that unlike venues in Japan and Singapore—the only Asian countries that are a part of the Eras Tour—those in Hong Kong, the Philippines, Indonesia, and Thailand might not have the proper infrastructure that is required to host the tour.[[29]](#cite_note-31) In addition, Hong Kong and mainland China [reopened to foreigners much later](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zero-COVID "Zero-COVID") than most other places following the [COVID-19 pandemic lockdowns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Hong_Kong "COVID-19 pandemic in Hong Kong"), which may have affected touring plans, as per *[South China Morning Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_China_Morning_Post "South China Morning Post")*.[[30]](#cite_note-32) In Malaysia, legal [opposition to LGBT rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Malaysia "LGBT rights in Malaysia"), Singapore exclusive agreement, and the ban on pop concerts during [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan "Ramadan") were cited as potential reasons.[[31]](#cite_note-33)[[32]](#cite_note-34)[[33]](#cite_note-35)

## Ticket sales

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=2 "Edit section: Ticket sales")]

Ticket sales for the Eras Tour were handled by various [ticketing agencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_ticket_outlet "Primary ticket outlet") around the world, such as [Ticketmaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ticketmaster "Ticketmaster"), over several dates. Met with unprecedented, record-setting demand, tickets at every venue sold out in hours.[[34]](#cite_note-20_Minuten-2023-36)[[35]](#cite_note-Ong-2023-37) Sales faced technical malfunctions in many countries; bulk purchases of tickets by [scalpers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scalping_(trading) "Scalping (trading)") and [bots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_bot "Internet bot") were reported at numerous venues, resulting in tickets being listed on [resale platforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ticket_resale "Ticket resale") for exorbitant prices.[[36]](#cite_note-Roeloffs-38)[[37]](#cite_note-AsianFans-BBC-39) [StubHub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/StubHub "StubHub") noted that the Eras Tour ticket sales were "tracking to be the best-selling of any artist [they've] seen".[[38]](#cite_note-40) Jay Marciano, CEO of AEG, stated that Swift outright refused to impose [dynamic pricing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynamic_pricing "Dynamic pricing") on the tickets in the purview of her fans.[[39]](#cite_note-41)

### US and Canada

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=3 "Edit section: US and Canada")]

See also: [Taylor Swift–Ticketmaster controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift–Ticketmaster_controversy "Taylor Swift–Ticketmaster controversy")

The first US leg was set to go on sale to the general public on November 18, 2022. Due to Swift's multi-year partnership with [Capital One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_One "Capital One"), their cardholders had [presale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presales "Presales") access, which was set to begin on November 15.[[40]](#cite_note-42) Fans registered for the Ticketmaster Verified Fan program from November 1 through 9 to receive a presale code that granted exclusive access to the TaylorSwiftTix Presale on November 15; those who purchased merchandise from Swift's website received "boosts", and previous Lover Fest ticket holders also received preferred access to the presale if they registered using the same Ticketmaster account.[[41]](#cite_note-43)[[42]](#cite_note-44) Swift confirmed ticket prices in advance, abandoning the "platinum ticket" model; they ranged from US$49 to $449, while VIP packages ranged from $199 to $899.[[4]](#cite_note-Willman-2022b-6) According to Ticketmaster, the TaylorSwiftTix Presale provided "the best opportunity to get more tickets into the hands of fans who want to attend the show" by evading bots and scalpers.[[43]](#cite_note-45) It also noted that if demand from the program "exceeds supply", it is possible that "verified fans may be selected at random to participate in the presale."[[44]](#cite_note-46) It later reported that it received a record 3.5 million verified fan registrations for the Eras Tour.[[45]](#cite_note-47) Ahead of the second leg's presale on August 11, Ticketmaster estimated that 14 million users were vying for roughly 625,000 tickets.[[46]](#cite_note-48)

Ticketmaster handled the Canadian sales as well, using the Verified Fan program.[[10]](#cite_note-Kreps-2023-12) Members of the [Royal Bank of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Bank_of_Canada "Royal Bank of Canada")'s Avion Rewards program received exclusive access to a separate presale.[[47]](#cite_note-49) An estimated 31 million people registered for the Toronto Verified Fan presale, equal to over 77 percent of Canada's population.[[48]](#cite_note-50)

### Latin America

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=4 "Edit section: Latin America")]

Latin American tickets went on sale in early June 2023.[[12]](#cite_note-Garcia-2023-14) Presale access was granted to [Banco Patagonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banco_Patagonia "Banco Patagonia") clients in Argentina on June 5;[[49]](#cite_note-51) around one million customers were reported to have queued for the 24,000 available presale tickets for the Buenos Aires shows,[[50]](#cite_note-52) and over three million during the general sale.[[51]](#cite_note-53)[[52]](#cite_note-54) DF Entertainment served as Swift's tour promotion partner in Argentina; CEO Diego Finkelstein called the demand "unprecedented",[[53]](#cite_note-55) based on which *[Perfil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perfil "Perfil")* opined that Swift could fill the stadium 36 times if she wanted to.[[54]](#cite_note-56) On the day of the show, more than 1 million users tried to get last-minute seats.[[55]](#cite_note-57)

In Mexico, ticket presale was handled by Ticketmaster's Verified Fan program; registrations ran from June 2 through June 7, followed by a general public on-sale.[[56]](#cite_note-58) In Brazil, previous Lover Fest ticket holders and C6 Bank [Mastercard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mastercard "Mastercard") holders gained access to presales on June 6 and 10, respectively. Instantly after the announcement of the shows on June 2, people camped outside [Allianz Parque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allianz_Parque "Allianz Parque") to purchase physical tickets to the São Paulo shows that would not go on sale to the general public until June 12.[[57]](#cite_note-59)[[58]](#cite_note-60) The Mastercard presale tickets sold out in 30 minutes, with over one million customers queueing.[[59]](#cite_note-61) On June 12, over two million users queued for the general sale online.[[60]](#cite_note-62)

### Asia-Pacific

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=5 "Edit section: Asia-Pacific")]

Tickets in Japan were sold by [Lawson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawson_(store) "Lawson (store)") under their [Loppi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loppi_(ticketing_system) "Loppi (ticketing system)") ticket system. Unlike other countries, all Japanese tickets were only sold as lottery tickets. The pre-sale for [American Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Express "American Express") card holders began from June 23 to 26, 2023, and pre-sale for Lawson began from June 27 to July 10.[[61]](#cite_note-63) Due to the high demand, Lawson announced the second lottery pre-sale, which began from July 28 to August 3.[[62]](#cite_note-64) The additional general sale took place on August 22, 2023, and immediately sold out.[[63]](#cite_note-65)

In Australia, tickets were only available via [Ticketek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ticketek "Ticketek").[[64]](#cite_note-66) *[Guardian Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guardian_Australia "Guardian Australia")* reported that the Australian leg was met with unprecedented demand as well.[[65]](#cite_note-Cassidy-2023-67) Within 12 hours, over one million people signed up for presale codes.[[66]](#cite_note-68) American Express cardholders purchased VIP packages from June 26 to 28; the website crashed within half an hour, and all packages sold out on June 26.[[67]](#cite_note-69) Following the scalping highlighted by Australian consumer organization [Choice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Choice_(Australian_consumer_organisation) "Choice (Australian consumer organisation)"), the [Victoria State Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_State_Government "Victoria State Government") declared the Eras Tour in Melbourne a "major event", a legal provision penalizing scalping and misleading advertisement in [Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_(state) "Victoria (state)"). Because reselling a ticket for more than 10% above the original price had already been illegal in [New South Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_South_Wales "New South Wales"), [the government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_New_South_Wales "Government of New South Wales") investigated the ticket resale platform [Viagogo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viagogo "Viagogo") after the scalping reports. On June 28, those registered with [Frontier Touring Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frontier_Touring_Company "Frontier Touring Company") gained access to a presale for which over four million users queued, setting a national record; tickets sold out within three hours. Ticketek stated it neutralized over 500 million bot purchase attempts during the presale. The public on-sale began on June 30, with tickets selling out that day.[[65]](#cite_note-Cassidy-2023-67)[[68]](#cite_note-70)[[69]](#cite_note-71) A second sale on November 10 sold out within two hours for Sydney and under one hour for Melbourne.[[70]](#cite_note-72) A third sale for a limited number of tickets, including restricted view seats, occurred on February 13, 2024.[[71]](#cite_note-73)

For the Singaporean shows, [United Overseas Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Overseas_Bank "United Overseas Bank") (UOB) cardholders in Singapore, Malaysia, Thailand, Indonesia and Vietnam had presale access on July 5, 2023, with over one million users in the virtual queue. *[The Straits Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Straits_Times "The Straits Times")* reported that Swift's fans in Singapore and other eligible [Southeast Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Asia "Southeast Asia") countries began "scrambling" to sign up for UOB cards.[[72]](#cite_note-74)[[73]](#cite_note-75) Around 22 million users registered for access to the 330,000 available tickets during the July 7 general sale, which happened both virtually and via [Singapore Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore_Post "Singapore Post") offices; tickets sold out immediately despite website crashes.[[35]](#cite_note-Ong-2023-37)[[37]](#cite_note-AsianFans-BBC-39)[[74]](#cite_note-76) Klook, an official experience partner for the Singapore leg, sold travel packages along with tickets, which were purchased by dozens of Filipino fans and instantly sold out.[[75]](#cite_note-77) [Marina Bay Sands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marina_Bay_Sands "Marina Bay Sands") sold tickets coupled with hotel stays and other experiences.[[76]](#cite_note-78)

### Europe

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=6 "Edit section: Europe")]

The [Lisbon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lisbon "Lisbon") shows went on sale on July 12 and 27, 2023, via [See Tickets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/See_Tickets "See Tickets"), with transferrable access codes mailed to registered users, who were limited to four tickets per code.[[77]](#cite_note-79)[[78]](#cite_note-80) Standard tickets for both shows sold out within 2.5 hours.[[79]](#cite_note-81) Following popular demand for an additional show in Madrid, Spain, La Liga approved football club Real Madrid's request to move their match from May 26 to May 25, 2024, to make way for a second Eras Tour show at the [Santiago Bernabéu Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santiago_Bernabéu_Stadium "Santiago Bernabéu Stadium") on May 29.[[80]](#cite_note-82)[[81]](#cite_note-83)

[![Taylor Swift performs Cruel Summer at the beginning of her final Era's Tour show in Warsaw, Poland.](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/Taylor-Swift-Eras01.jpg/250px-Taylor-Swift-Eras01.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor-Swift-Eras01.jpg)

Swift performs "Cruel Summer" in Warsaw, Poland

Three million people queued for tickets in Germany,[[82]](#cite_note-84) and 600,000 registered for the [Warsaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw "Warsaw") shows.[[83]](#cite_note-85) All 170,000 tickets for the three [Vienna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vienna "Vienna") shows sold out within a few hours, marking the largest and fastest ticket sale ever in Austria.[[84]](#cite_note-ORF_Topos-2023-86) All 95,000 [Zürich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zürich "Zürich") tickets sold out within 30 minutes.[[34]](#cite_note-20_Minuten-2023-36) In Italy, two million people tried to access tickets for the two shows in Milan, per Italian organizers D'Alessandro & Galli.[[85]](#cite_note-87)

In France, [TF1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TF1 "TF1") reported the highest demand ever for a presale. Angelo Gopee, head of Live Nation France, stated, "the demand is such that many have found themselves in a virtual queue just to subscribe to the mailing list which, potentially, will open access to the ticket office. From memory, we had never seen that in France".[[86]](#cite_note-88) The Paris presale opened on July 11, 2023, to over one million queued users. Ticketmaster suspended both Paris and [Lyon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyon "Lyon") presales within an hour after reports of [login](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Login "Login") glitches;[[87]](#cite_note-89) they were rescheduled to July 17 to 21.[[88]](#cite_note-90) Over 250,000 tickets were sold for the six French dates, with AEG France executive Arnaud Meersseman estimating Swift could have done twelve shows due to the overwhelming demand.[[89]](#cite_note-91) According to [RMC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RMC_(France) "RMC (France)"), the capacity for the four shows at [Paris La Défense Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_La_Défense_Arena "Paris La Défense Arena") was also increased from 41,500 to 45,000, meaning Swift will perform for 180,000 attendees in Paris alone.[[90]](#cite_note-RMC-2024-92)

Ticketmaster and [AXS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AXS_(company) "AXS (company)") handled the United Kingdom sale.[[91]](#cite_note-93) Those who had previously pre-ordered *Midnights* were able to access presales from July 10 to 12.[[36]](#cite_note-Roeloffs-38) London's [Wembley Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wembley_Stadium "Wembley Stadium") officials described the demand for tickets as "unprecedented" and noted that waiting times were "longer than usual".[[92]](#cite_note-94) [Cardiff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiff "Cardiff")'s [Principality Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_Stadium "Principality Stadium") tickets were pre-sold on July 14. In addition to website malfunctions, *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes "Forbes")* reported widespread scalping of the tour's UK tickets, with immediate re-listing on sites like StubHub and Viagogo for extortionate prices.[[93]](#cite_note-95)[[36]](#cite_note-Roeloffs-38) Viagogo responded that the demand of the tour's European leg is something the company had not seen "since the Beatles".[[94]](#cite_note-96) The UK general sale took place from July 17 to 19.[[95]](#cite_note-97) In Scotland, the [City of Edinburgh Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Edinburgh_Council "City of Edinburgh Council") granted organizers permission to increase [Murrayfield Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murrayfield_Stadium "Murrayfield Stadium")'s capacity from 67,130 to 72,990 for Swift's three shows.[[96]](#cite_note-98) In Ireland, around 500,000 people registered for the [Dublin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dublin "Dublin") shows.[[97]](#cite_note-99) On account of the website crashes during the UK sale, Ticketmaster announced staggered on-sale times for Dublin on July 20, 2023; tickets sold out in minutes. *[The Irish Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Irish_Times "The Irish Times")* highlighted that, unlike the UK, "the practice of reselling tickets above face value has been illegal in Ireland since 2021", so no scalping was reported.[[98]](#cite_note-100)

## Production

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=7 "Edit section: Production")]

The Eras Tour was produced by Swift's in-house tour production company, Taylor Swift Touring.[[99]](#cite_note-101) The company hired around 90 trucks to haul staging, costumes and other equipment for the shows.[[100]](#cite_note-102) Canadian production designer [Ethan Tobman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethan_Tobman "Ethan Tobman") served as the tour's [creative director](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creative_director "Creative director").[[101]](#cite_note-Gensler-2023-103) *[The Wall Street Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wall_Street_Journal "The Wall Street Journal")* stated the tour was one of the most expensive and "technically ambitious" productions of the 21st century.[[102]](#cite_note-Shah-2023-104) [Interior design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interior_design "Interior design") magazine *[Architectural Digest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architectural_Digest "Architectural Digest")* named the tour Swift's "most ambitious" [set design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Set_design "Set design") and praised its [worldbuilding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worldbuilding "Worldbuilding").[[103]](#cite_note-McLaughlin-2023-105)

### Stage and lighting

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=8 "Edit section: Stage and lighting")]

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/92/Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_-_Singapore_National_Stadium_-_9_Mar_2024%281%29.jpeg/120px-Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_-_Singapore_National_Stadium_-_9_Mar_2024%281%29.jpeg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_-_Singapore_National_Stadium_-_9_Mar_2024(1).jpeg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_Minnesota_-_Midnights_act_11.jpg/120px-Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_Minnesota_-_Midnights_act_11.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis,_Minnesota_-_Midnights_act_11.jpg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_MN_-_Red_act_-_3.jpg/120px-Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_MN_-_Red_act_-_3.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis,_MN_-_Red_act_-_3.jpg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_Minnesota_-_Midnights_act_4.jpg/120px-Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_Minnesota_-_Midnights_act_4.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis,_Minnesota_-_Midnights_act_4.jpg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_MN_-_1989_act_12.jpg/120px-Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_MN_-_1989_act_12.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis,_MN_-_1989_act_12.jpg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_MN_-_Fearless_act_-_2.jpg/120px-Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_MN_-_Fearless_act_-_2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis,_MN_-_Fearless_act_-_2.jpg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_MN_-_Speak_Now_act_-_2.jpg/120px-Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_MN_-_Speak_Now_act_-_2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis,_MN_-_Speak_Now_act_-_2.jpg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ad/Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_Minnesota_-_Midnights_act_2.jpg/120px-Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis%2C_Minnesota_-_Midnights_act_2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Eras_Tour_-_Minneapolis,_Minnesota_-_Midnights_act_2.jpg)

The production consists of light beams, smoke machines, [confetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confetti "Confetti") shooters, coordinated serial lights, [pyrotechnics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyrotechnics "Pyrotechnics"), and hydraulic platforms.[[104]](#cite_note-Pollstar-2023a-106)[[105]](#cite_note-Raggio-107)[[106]](#cite_note-McCormick-2023-108) The middle stage has mobile blocks that form different shapes of elevated platform throughout the show.[[107]](#cite_note-Lipshutz-2023-109)

The Eras Tour staging was expansive, made of digital displays. It consisted of three separate stages connected by a broad ramp:[[108]](#cite_note-Cohen-2023-110) a main stage with a giant, curved [widescreen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Widescreen "Widescreen"),[[109]](#cite_note-Young-2023-111)[[110]](#cite_note-Willman-2023c-112) a [rhombic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhombus "Rhombus") middle stage,[[107]](#cite_note-Lipshutz-2023-109) and a rectangular stage that along with the ramp forms a T-shape at the middle of the floor.[[111]](#cite_note-Horton-2023-113) The stages featured various visuals and effects throughout the show.[[112]](#cite_note-Procell-2023-114) The tour's concept centered on worldbuilding, featuring a diverse set of props and performing styles to convey the varying moods and aesthetics of Swift's albums.[[113]](#cite_note-Aramesh-2023-115)[[111]](#cite_note-Horton-2023-113)

The staging was equipped with an in-built "hyperactive" [hydraulic platform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolving_stage "Revolving stage"),[[106]](#cite_note-McCormick-2023-108) with the main and middle stages having mobile blocks that manually rose from the center to form platforms of different shapes,[[107]](#cite_note-Lipshutz-2023-109) and another car-like platform that was moved on the middle stage, driven by an operator seated within the platform but concealed from the audience.[[114]](#cite_note-116) The tour's "massive" production is heavily inspired by [Broadway theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Broadway_theatre "Broadway theatre").[[111]](#cite_note-Horton-2023-113)[[115]](#cite_note-117)[[113]](#cite_note-Aramesh-2023-115) It was described as a [4D cognitive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/4D_film "4D film") experience,[[109]](#cite_note-Young-2023-111) featuring [pyrotechnics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pyrotechnics "Pyrotechnics"), [laser lights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laser_lighting_display "Laser lighting display"),[[105]](#cite_note-Raggio-107) smoke machines, fire cannons,[[104]](#cite_note-Pollstar-2023a-106) indoor [fireworks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fireworks "Fireworks"), [PixMob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PixMob "PixMob") LED bracelets,[[106]](#cite_note-McCormick-2023-108) and [image projection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Projector "Projector") technology,[[108]](#cite_note-Cohen-2023-110) such as [projection mapping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Projection_mapping "Projection mapping").[[109]](#cite_note-Young-2023-111)

### Music and performance

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=9 "Edit section: Music and performance")]

Designed as a tribute to [Swift's discography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift_albums_discography "Taylor Swift albums discography") across her career since 2006, the Eras Tour covered all of the styles of music seen in her 11 studio albums, including *[The Tortured Poets Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Tortured_Poets_Department "The Tortured Poets Department")*, which was added in the European leg of the tour after the album's release. The styles ranged from [country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_music "Country music") and [pop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_music "Pop music") to [folk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contemporary_folk_music "Contemporary folk music") and [alternative rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_rock "Alternative rock") genres.[[116]](#cite_note-Barnes-2023-118) Some media outlets dubbed the Eras Tour the "[greatest hits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greatest_hits "Greatest hits")" tour of an artist still in her commercial prime.[[117]](#cite_note-119)[[113]](#cite_note-Aramesh-2023-115) The [set list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Set_list "Set list") placed a somewhat greater focus on albums Swift had not previously toured, including first-ever live performances of several tracks.[[118]](#cite_note-120) According to the music journalist [Alexis Petridis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexis_Petridis "Alexis Petridis"), the Eras Tour was a [musically eclectic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eclecticism_in_music "Eclecticism in music") production, consisting of "dubstep-inspired, dark-hued pop; tweedy folk; monster-chorus-sporting anthems and acoustic guitar-driven songs that show her Nashville grounding".[[119]](#cite_note-Petridis-2024-121)

The concert ensemble included 15 [backup dancers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Backup_dancer "Backup dancer"),[[120]](#cite_note-122) choreographed by [Mandy Moore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandy_Moore_(choreographer) "Mandy Moore (choreographer)"),[[121]](#cite_note-123) and Swift's live band of six instrumentalists who have been touring with her since 2007 and four female [backing vocalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Backing_vocalist "Backing vocalist").[[122]](#cite_note-124) Moore was recommended by Swift's friend, American actress [Emma Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emma_Stone "Emma Stone"), who had worked with Moore on the musical film *[La La Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_La_Land "La La Land")* (2016).[[123]](#cite_note-125) To prepare for the tour physically, Swift trained using a custom workout routine designed by personal trainer [Kirk Myers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirk_Myers "Kirk Myers");[[124]](#cite_note-126) Swift sang the entire set list daily while running on a treadmill, trained in dance for three months leading up to the first show, and forwent the consumption of alcohol.[[125]](#cite_note-127)

### Fashion

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=10 "Edit section: Fashion")]

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Eras_Tour_-_Arlington%2C_TX_-_Reputation_act_1_%28cropped%29.jpg/120px-Eras_Tour_-_Arlington%2C_TX_-_Reputation_act_1_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Eras_Tour_-_Arlington,_TX_-_Reputation_act_1_(cropped).jpg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Taylor_Swift_performing_%22Enchanted%22_on_The_Eras_Tour_%28Tampa%2C_FL_-_Raymond_Jaymes_Stadium_-_April_2023%29.jpg/120px-Taylor_Swift_performing_%22Enchanted%22_on_The_Eras_Tour_%28Tampa%2C_FL_-_Raymond_Jaymes_Stadium_-_April_2023%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_performing_)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4d/Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_The_Folklore_Set_Era_%2853108930977%29.jpg/120px-Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_The_Folklore_Set_Era_%2853108930977%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_The_Folklore_Set_Era_(53108930977).jpg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington%2C_TX_-_Midnights_act_%28cropped%29.jpg/120px-Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington%2C_TX_-_Midnights_act_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington,_TX_-_Midnights_act_(cropped).jpg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/43/Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Lover_Set_%2853109595289%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/120px-Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Lover_Set_%2853109595289%29_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Lover_Set_(53109595289)_(cropped).jpg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Fearless_Set_Era_%2853109821975%29.jpg/120px-Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Fearless_Set_Era_%2853109821975%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Fearless_Set_Era_(53109821975).jpg)

Designers such as [Roberto Cavalli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Cavalli "Roberto Cavalli"), [Zuhair Murad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zuhair_Murad "Zuhair Murad") and [Oscar de la Renta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_de_la_Renta "Oscar de la Renta") custom-made Swift's outfits for the tour to complement each album's aesthetic.[[126]](#cite_note-Regan-2023-128)[[127]](#cite_note-Allaire-2023-129)

Swift's wardrobe at the Eras Tour was a subject of widespread media coverage.[[128]](#cite_note-Friedman-2023-130) Her costumes and those of her accompanying dancers, as well as her microphones and guitars, paid homage to her 10 albums.[[127]](#cite_note-Allaire-2023-129)[[129]](#cite_note-131) They were inspired by previous performances, music videos, and public appearances,[[130]](#cite_note-Cosores-2023-132)[[131]](#cite_note-Wong-2023-133) intended to align with the overarching themes and [palette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Color_scheme "Color scheme") of the era Swift referenced in an act, showcasing the various sonic and visual aesthetics she had adopted in her career.[[127]](#cite_note-Allaire-2023-129)[[131]](#cite_note-Wong-2023-133) However, [crystals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crystal "Crystal") were a unifying fashion choice; costumes of every act were adorned with them.[[132]](#cite_note-Grindell-2023-134)

Attire and accessories were mostly custom-made by fashion houses Swift had worked with before, such as [Atelier Versace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Versace "Versace"), [Roberto Cavalli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Cavalli_(company) "Roberto Cavalli (company)"), [Etro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etro "Etro"), Nicole + Felicia Couture, [Zuhair Murad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zuhair_Murad "Zuhair Murad"), [Elie Saab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elie_Saab "Elie Saab"), Ashish, [Alberta Ferretti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberta_Ferretti "Alberta Ferretti"), Jessica Jones, and [Oscar de la Renta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_de_la_Renta "Oscar de la Renta").[[126]](#cite_note-Regan-2023-128)[[127]](#cite_note-Allaire-2023-129)[[128]](#cite_note-Friedman-2023-130) Swift wore variations of some costumes at different shows, usually paired with [Christian Louboutin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Louboutin "Christian Louboutin") shoes.[[128]](#cite_note-Friedman-2023-130) Swift's black [fedora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fedora "Fedora") from "[22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/22_(Taylor_Swift_song) "22 (Taylor Swift song)")" was designed by [Gladys Tamez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gladys_Tamez "Gladys Tamez").[[133]](#cite_note-135) Fausto Puglisi, a designer for Roberto Cavalli, stated that he took an "artisanal approach to craftsmanship" while customizing the outfits, claiming "everything must be eye-catching" when designing for concerts.[[127]](#cite_note-Allaire-2023-129) He incorporated [Swarovski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swarovski "Swarovski") crystals in the costumes Swift wore during the *[Fearless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fearless_(Taylor_Swift_album) "Fearless (Taylor Swift album)")*, *[1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_(Taylor_Swift_album) "1989 (Taylor Swift album)")*, and *Reputation* acts, which required over 170 hours of meticulous "hand-craftsmanship by skilled artisans" to make.[[134]](#cite_note-136) The [sequined](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sequin "Sequin") [tulle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tulle_(netting) "Tulle (netting)") ball gown that Murad designed for the *Speak Now* act required "over 350 hours of [atelier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atelier "Atelier") handwork". Ferretti used [chiffon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiffon_(fabric) "Chiffon (fabric)") and micro-beading for the *Folklore* era dresses, while the *Midnights* Oscar de la Renta fringed bodysuit was hand-adorned with more than 5,300 beads and crystals.[[132]](#cite_note-Grindell-2023-134)

*[StyleCaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/StyleCaster "StyleCaster")* regarded the Eras Tour wardrobe as Swift's best fashion collection for a tour.[[135]](#cite_note-137) *The New York Times*' chief fashion critic [Vanessa Friedman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanessa_Friedman "Vanessa Friedman") considered the Eras Tour a [fashion show](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fashion_show "Fashion show") beyond merely live music and praised Swift's "fabulous" but purposeful wardrobe choices for setting the bar high for future concerts by other artists.[[128]](#cite_note-Friedman-2023-130)

## Concert synopsis

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=11 "Edit section: Concert synopsis")]

[![Lover act of the Eras Tour](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Lover_Set_%2853108816372%29.jpg/250px-Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Lover_Set_%2853108816372%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Lover_Set_(53108816372).jpg)

[![Fearless act of the Eras Tour](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Fearless_Set_Era_%2853109813215%29.jpg/250px-Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Fearless_Set_Era_%2853109813215%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Fearless_Set_Era_(53109813215).jpg)

Swift performing with her band during the first two acts: *[Lover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lover_(album) "Lover (album)")* (top) and *[Fearless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fearless_(Taylor's_Version) "Fearless (Taylor's Version)")* (bottom)

A typical show of the Eras Tour lasted 3 hours and 15 minutes. It consisted of between 44 and 46 songs, grouped into 10 acts, representing each "musical era" of her discography. Each act was characterized by a specific [color scheme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Color_scheme "Color scheme"), costume, and stage design, and transitions between acts were facilitated by on-screen interlude visuals and marked by costume changes with negligible [intermissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intermission "Intermission").[[136]](#cite_note-138)

### March 2023 to March 2024

The show commenced with the ***[Lover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lover_(album) "Lover (album)")*** act. A clock on-screen counted down to show time as Lesley Gore's "[You Don't Own Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You_Don't_Own_Me "You Don't Own Me")" (1964) played.[[137]](#cite_note-Ruggieri-2023-139) Surrounded by dancers in pastel-colored, fan-like tapestry,[[103]](#cite_note-McLaughlin-2023-105) Swift emerged from the platform at mid-stage in a bodysuit and boots.[[138]](#cite_note-Yahr-2023-140)[[113]](#cite_note-Aramesh-2023-115) She opened the show with the chorus of "[Miss Americana & the Heartbreak Prince](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miss_Americana_&_the_Heartbreak_Prince "Miss Americana & the Heartbreak Prince")" leading into "[Cruel Summer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruel_Summer_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Cruel Summer (Taylor Swift song)")".[[139]](#cite_note-Lewis-2023-141) Accompanied by dancers, Swift performed "[The Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Man_(Taylor_Swift_song) "The Man (Taylor Swift song)")" and "[You Need to Calm Down](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You_Need_to_Calm_Down "You Need to Calm Down")" in a sequined [blazer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blazer "Blazer"), through a set emulating office [cubicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cubicle "Cubicle"). She then delivered the welcome note with the dollhouse from the "[Lover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lover_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Lover (Taylor Swift song)")" music video on-screen, depicting her various album eras.[[103]](#cite_note-McLaughlin-2023-105) She played an acoustic version of "Lover" on the guitar with her band, backup singers, and dancers,[[140]](#cite_note-Wood-2023-142)[[141]](#cite_note-Bream-2023-143) followed by a stripped-down rendition of "[The Archer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Archer_(song) "The Archer (song)")" alone on the ramp.[[113]](#cite_note-Aramesh-2023-115) The second act, ***[Fearless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fearless_(Taylor's_Version) "Fearless (Taylor's Version)")***, began with the screen showing gold [electric sparks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_spark "Electric spark") raining down. Swift reappeared in a metallic fringed dress and country boots.[[138]](#cite_note-Yahr-2023-140) She performed "[Fearless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fearless_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Fearless (Taylor Swift song)")" on the main stage, "[You Belong with Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You_Belong_with_Me "You Belong with Me")" at mid-stage, and "[Love Story](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_Story_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Love Story (Taylor Swift song)")" on the T-stage, all alongside her band while her backup singers wore metallic gold and silver jackets and black jumpsuits.[[107]](#cite_note-Lipshutz-2023-109)

[![Evermore act of the Eras Tour](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Evermore_Era_Set_%2853108854802%29.jpg/250px-Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Evermore_Era_Set_%2853108854802%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Evermore_Era_Set_(53108854802).jpg)

[![Reputation act of the Eras Tour](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington_TX_20230331_-_Look_What_You_Made_Me_Do_1.jpg/250px-Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington_TX_20230331_-_Look_What_You_Made_Me_Do_1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington_TX_20230331_-_Look_What_You_Made_Me_Do_1.jpg)

Swift performing "[Willow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willow_(song) "Willow (song)")" and "[Look What You Made Me Do](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Look_What_You_Made_Me_Do "Look What You Made Me Do")" as part of the *[Evermore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evermore "Evermore")* (top) and *[Reputation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reputation_(album) "Reputation (album)")* (bottom) acts

The third act, ***[Evermore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evermore "Evermore")***, adopted a forest aesthetic.[[116]](#cite_note-Barnes-2023-118) Swift began singing "['Tis the Damn Season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/'Tis_the_Damn_Season "'Tis the Damn Season")" in a long gown,[[138]](#cite_note-Yahr-2023-140) followed by a dark theme that leads to "[Willow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willow_(song) "Willow (song)")" in a "witchy" [séance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Séance "Séance");[[137]](#cite_note-Ruggieri-2023-139) she wore a cape and performed with dancers holding luminescent orange orbs.[[130]](#cite_note-Cosores-2023-132)[[138]](#cite_note-Yahr-2023-140) She continued with "[Marjorie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marjorie_(song) "Marjorie (song)")",[[107]](#cite_note-Lipshutz-2023-109) then "[Champagne Problems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champagne_Problems_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Champagne Problems (Taylor Swift song)")" on a moss-covered piano beneath an oak tree,[[116]](#cite_note-Barnes-2023-118)[[103]](#cite_note-McLaughlin-2023-105) concluding the act with Swift introducing her pianist Karina DePiano followed by "[Tolerate It](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tolerate_It "Tolerate It")" with a male dancer at a dinner table reminiscent of *[Citizen Kane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citizen_Kane "Citizen Kane")* (1941).[[142]](#cite_note-144) Snakes visuals and dimming lights started the ***[Reputation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reputation_(album) "Reputation (album)")*** act.[[103]](#cite_note-McLaughlin-2023-105)[[130]](#cite_note-Cosores-2023-132) Swift reemerged in a black asymmetrical [catsuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catsuit "Catsuit") with snake motifs.[[143]](#cite_note-145) She delivered a high-energy performance of "[...Ready for It?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/...Ready_for_It%3F "...Ready for It?")" with female dancers wearing black-dark red gothic [leotards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leotard "Leotard"), "[Delicate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delicate_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Delicate (Taylor Swift song)")",[[107]](#cite_note-Lipshutz-2023-109)[[111]](#cite_note-Horton-2023-113) and "[Don't Blame Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don't_Blame_Me_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Don't Blame Me (Taylor Swift song)")" featured light beams and Swift on an elevated platform.[[144]](#cite_note-146) She transitioned to "[Look What You Made Me Do](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Look_What_You_Made_Me_Do "Look What You Made Me Do")", which featured on-screen visuals of Swift from all of her eras trapped in glass boxes and dancers dressed in some of Swift's older looks.[[130]](#cite_note-Cosores-2023-132)[[116]](#cite_note-Barnes-2023-118) The act concluded with a snake and elephant slithering away on-screen.[[112]](#cite_note-Procell-2023-114)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6a/Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_The_Red_Era_Set_%2853109768961%29.jpg/250px-Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_The_Red_Era_Set_%2853109768961%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_The_Red_Era_Set_(53109768961).jpg)

For the *[Red](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_(Taylor's_Version) "Red (Taylor's Version)")* act, Swift wore a custom [Gladys Tamez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gladys_Tamez "Gladys Tamez") black fedora hat that she presented to a fan from the audience while performing "[22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/22_(Taylor_Swift_song) "22 (Taylor Swift song)")".

The fifth act, ***[Speak Now](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speak_Now_(Taylor's_Version) "Speak Now (Taylor's Version)")***, began with an abstract [mosaic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mosaic "Mosaic") of purple lights on the stage.[[145]](#cite_note-Carroll-2023-147) Swift, in a [ball gown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ball_gown "Ball gown"), walked in from the screen and performed "[Enchanted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enchanted_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Enchanted (Taylor Swift song)")" with female dancers; since July 7, 2023, she also performed "[Long Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Live_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Long Live (Taylor Swift song)")" with her band.[[107]](#cite_note-Lipshutz-2023-109) The color scheme changed to match the next act, ***[Red](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_(Taylor's_Version) "Red (Taylor's Version)")***; a female dancer opened a box that plays snippets of "[Red](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Red (Taylor Swift song)")", "[Everything Has Changed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Everything_Has_Changed "Everything Has Changed")" or "[Holy Ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Ground_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Holy Ground (Taylor Swift song)")", and "[State of Grace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Grace_(song) "State of Grace (song)")".[[137]](#cite_note-Ruggieri-2023-139) Red balloons emerged, and Swift performed "[22](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/22_(Taylor_Swift_song) "22 (Taylor Swift song)")" wearing a version of the white T-shirt and black hat from the song's music video.[[106]](#cite_note-McCormick-2023-108) Near the end of the song, she greeted a fan pre-selected from the audience, giving them the hat.[[146]](#cite_note-148) Swift sang "[We Are Never Ever Getting Back Together](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/We_Are_Never_Ever_Getting_Back_Together "We Are Never Ever Getting Back Together")" and "[I Knew You Were Trouble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Knew_You_Were_Trouble "I Knew You Were Trouble")" with male dancers next, dressed in a red-black romper.[[131]](#cite_note-Wong-2023-133) She donned a red-black ombre coat next, and performed "[All Too Well (10 Minute Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_Too_Well_(10_Minute_Version) "All Too Well (10 Minute Version)")" on a guitar, backed by the band.[[106]](#cite_note-McCormick-2023-108)[[131]](#cite_note-Wong-2023-133) The act concluded with artificial snow falling.[[107]](#cite_note-Lipshutz-2023-109)

[![Taylor Swift singing while sitting on moss, dressed in a floating dress](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ee/Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington%2C_TX_-_Folklore_act_2.jpg/250px-Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington%2C_TX_-_Folklore_act_2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington,_TX_-_Folklore_act_2.jpg)

Swift in a frilly [Alberta Ferretti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberta_Ferretti "Alberta Ferretti") gown for the *[Folklore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore_(Taylor_Swift_album) "Folklore (Taylor Swift album)")* act, complementing the album's [cottagecore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cottagecore "Cottagecore") aesthetic

[Cottagecore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cottagecore "Cottagecore") dominated the seventh act, ***[Folklore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore_(Taylor_Swift_album) "Folklore (Taylor Swift album)")***, introduced with a [spoken-word](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spoken-word "Spoken-word") interlude of "[Seven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seven_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Seven (Taylor Swift song)")".[[145]](#cite_note-Carroll-2023-147) Onstage is a bucolic, [A-frame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A-frame "A-frame") cabin setup, similar to the one from Swift's performance at the [63rd Annual Grammy Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/63rd_Annual_Grammy_Awards "63rd Annual Grammy Awards") (2021),[[139]](#cite_note-Lewis-2023-141)[[141]](#cite_note-Bream-2023-143) on an elevated platform with a staircase.[[111]](#cite_note-Horton-2023-113)[[103]](#cite_note-McLaughlin-2023-105) Swift performed "[Invisible String](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invisible_String "Invisible String")" or "[The 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_1 "The 1")" on the cabin's roof, "[Betty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Betty_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Betty (Taylor Swift song)")" with her band, and "[The Last Great American Dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Last_Great_American_Dynasty "The Last Great American Dynasty")" with dancers dressed in period clothing.[[138]](#cite_note-Yahr-2023-140) She then sang "[August](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_(song) "August (song)")", which transitioned to the bridge of a rock-tinged "[Illicit Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illicit_Affairs "Illicit Affairs")",[[108]](#cite_note-Cohen-2023-110)[[145]](#cite_note-Carroll-2023-147) followed by "[My Tears Ricochet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/My_Tears_Ricochet "My Tears Ricochet")" with female dancers wearing sparkly black long sleeved dresses and choreography resembling a [funeral procession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funeral_procession "Funeral procession").[[110]](#cite_note-Willman-2023c-112) Swift returned to the cabin to perform "[Cardigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardigan_(song) "Cardigan (song)")", ending the act with fireflies as the cabin retreated.[[138]](#cite_note-Yahr-2023-140)

***[1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_(Taylor's_Version) "1989 (Taylor's Version)")***, the eighth act, commenced with the screen showing a [neon-lit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neon_lighting "Neon lighting") city [skyline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skyline "Skyline").[[112]](#cite_note-Procell-2023-114) Swift, wearing a [crop top](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crop_top "Crop top") and skirt, sang "[Style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Style_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Style (Taylor Swift song)")" with dancers dressed in black-and-white outfits.[[113]](#cite_note-Aramesh-2023-115) Moving to mid-stage, female dancers rode neon bicycles for "[Blank Space](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blank_Space "Blank Space")" and used blue [golf clubs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golf_club "Golf club") to smash an animated [Shelby Cobra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shelby_Cobra "Shelby Cobra") car.[[107]](#cite_note-Lipshutz-2023-109)[[103]](#cite_note-McLaughlin-2023-105) She followed with "[Shake It Off](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shake_It_Off "Shake It Off")", performed as a robust dance party; "[Wildest Dreams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildest_Dreams "Wildest Dreams")", backed with backup singers and clips of a couple in bed; and "[Bad Blood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bad_Blood_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Bad Blood (Taylor Swift song)")", with female dancers and accentuated by intense pyrotechnics.[[138]](#cite_note-Yahr-2023-140) It was followed by the **acoustic set**, where Swift performed two surprise songs on guitar and piano.[[147]](#cite_note-Iasimone-2024-149) In an [optical illusion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Optical_illusion "Optical illusion"), a body of water developed around the piano and enveloped the stage; Swift then dove into the stage and appeared to swim underwater, along the ramp and toward the main stage.[[148]](#cite_note-150)

[![Taylor Swift performs on stage with three purple clouds around her](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington_TX_20230331_-_Lavender_Haze.jpg/250px-Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington_TX_20230331_-_Lavender_Haze.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington_TX_20230331_-_Lavender_Haze.jpg)

[![Taylor Swift in a sparkling purple dress performing a chair dance choreography onstage](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington_TX_20230331_-_Vigilante_Shit.jpg/250px-Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington_TX_20230331_-_Vigilante_Shit.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_-_Arlington_TX_20230331_-_Vigilante_Shit.jpg)

Swift performing "[Lavender Haze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lavender_Haze "Lavender Haze")" and "[Vigilante Shit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vigilante_Shit "Vigilante Shit")" during the *Midnights* act

The final act, ***[Midnights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midnights "Midnights")***, began with a wave from the illusion crashing against the screen; Swift woke up from a bed and climbed a ladder into a cloud. The lower screen split, and dancers carried out clouds as Swift reemerged in a purple [faux fur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faux_fur "Faux fur") coat, a glittery oversized shirt, and dark blue boots to sing "[Lavender Haze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lavender_Haze "Lavender Haze")".[[138]](#cite_note-Yahr-2023-140) She removed the coat and performed "[Anti-Hero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Hero_(song) "Anti-Hero (song)")" alongside a video of herself as a creature terrorizing a city.[[107]](#cite_note-Lipshutz-2023-109) Dancers performed with umbrellas as Swift sang "[Midnight Rain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midnight_Rain "Midnight Rain")" and changed costumes,[[138]](#cite_note-Yahr-2023-140) reappeared in a rhinestone-adorned [midnight blue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midnight_blue "Midnight blue") bodysuit.[[111]](#cite_note-Horton-2023-113) She then performed a "chair dance" choreographed for "[Vigilante Shit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vigilante_Shit "Vigilante Shit")",[[107]](#cite_note-Lipshutz-2023-109) influenced by "sultry" [burlesque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burlesque "Burlesque")[[139]](#cite_note-Lewis-2023-141) and the 1975 musical *[Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_(musical) "Chicago (musical)")*.[[137]](#cite_note-Ruggieri-2023-139) Swift followed with "[Bejeweled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bejeweled_(song) "Bejeweled (song)")", featuring moves inspired by the song's viral [TikTok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TikTok "TikTok") dance,[[149]](#cite_note-151) and "[Mastermind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mastermind_(song) "Mastermind (song)")" with the entire dance crew wearing black robes.[[116]](#cite_note-Barnes-2023-118) "[Karma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karma_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Karma (Taylor Swift song)")" was performed last, with Swift, backup singers, and dancers in tinsel-fringed jackets, closing the show with fireworks, colorful visuals, and confetti.[[139]](#cite_note-Lewis-2023-141)[[138]](#cite_note-Yahr-2023-140)

### May to December 2024

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=12 "Edit section: May to December 2024")]

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Taylor_Swift_TTPD_Dress.jpg/250px-Taylor_Swift_TTPD_Dress.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_TTPD_Dress.jpg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_TTPD_Set_Fortnight.jpg/250px-Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_TTPD_Set_Fortnight.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_Eras_Tour_TTPD_Set_Fortnight.jpg)

Swift performing "[Who's Afraid of Little Old Me?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Who's_Afraid_of_Little_Old_Me%3F "Who's Afraid of Little Old Me?")" on a moving glass-plated block (top) and "[Fortnight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortnight_(song) "Fortnight (song)")" on a bed emblazoned with the album's logo (bottom) for *[The Tortured Poets Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Tortured_Poets_Department "The Tortured Poets Department")* act

Starting from May 2024, Swift revamped the set list to include a new act with songs from her eleventh studio album, *[The Tortured Poets Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Tortured_Poets_Department "The Tortured Poets Department"),* released on April 19, 2024. "The Archer", "Long Live", "The 1", "The Last Great American Dynasty", "'Tis the Damn Season", and "Tolerate It" were removed from their respective acts to accommodate the album.[[150]](#cite_note-152) The *Speak Now* and *Red* acts were brought forward as the fourth and third acts, while the acts for *Folklore* and *Evermore*, which are considered "sister albums", merged into one. *The Tortured Poets Department* act was placed between the *1989* act and acoustic set.[[151]](#cite_note-Shafer-2024-153)

***The Tortured Poets Department*** act featured predominantly black-and-white graphics,[[151]](#cite_note-Shafer-2024-153) drawing from [dark academia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dark_academia "Dark academia").[[152]](#cite_note-Green-2024-154) It began with the screen showing pieces of furniture descending from the sky alongside paper pieces, which then transform into a deserted road and the furniture crash down.[[151]](#cite_note-Shafer-2024-153) Swift appeared onstage in a white corset dress with handwritten lyrics and sings "[But Daddy I Love Him](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/But_Daddy_I_Love_Him "But Daddy I Love Him")" and parts of "[So High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/So_High_School "So High School")", alongside dancers dressed in white.[[151]](#cite_note-Shafer-2024-153)[[153]](#cite_note-Georgi-2024-155) She performed "[Who's Afraid of Little Old Me?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Who's_Afraid_of_Little_Old_Me%3F "Who's Afraid of Little Old Me?")" on a moving glass-plated block and appeared to levitate in an illusion, which ended with the screen depicting an evil-possessed Swift. A [UFO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UFO "UFO") then appeared on screen and attempted to abduct Swift, who started singing "[Down Bad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Down_Bad_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Down Bad (Taylor Swift song)")" on the moving block that hovered over a digital [galaxy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galaxy "Galaxy").[[154]](#cite_note-156)[[152]](#cite_note-Green-2024-154) Transitioning to "[Fortnight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortnight_(song) "Fortnight (song)")", the stage featured a "TTPD"-emblazoned bed and two female dancers dressed as nurses. Swift sang the song as she sat on a typewriter, across from a male dancer.[[151]](#cite_note-Shafer-2024-153) She proceeded with "[The Smallest Man Who Ever Lived](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Smallest_Man_Who_Ever_Lived "The Smallest Man Who Ever Lived")", for which she put on a white marching band jacket as dancers marched beside her with drums.[[151]](#cite_note-Shafer-2024-153) The act ended with a silent skit with two male dancers accompanied by enthusiastic jazz music, where Swift removed her dress to reveal a two-piece bodysuit, leading up to "[I Can Do It with a Broken Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Can_Do_It_with_a_Broken_Heart "I Can Do It with a Broken Heart")".[[153]](#cite_note-Georgi-2024-155)[[155]](#cite_note-157)

At the end of the last shows of the tour, Swift exited the stage in the direction of a bright orange door on the screen.[[c]](#cite_note-159)

## Critical reception

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=13 "Edit section: Critical reception")]

> It's easy to compare one of Swift's stadium shows to something you'd see on Broadway—never has that been more true than for The Eras Tour. The setlist is cut up into acts, grouped by eras for each of Swift's [ten studio albums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift_albums_discography#Studio_albums "Taylor Swift albums discography"). For each era/act, Swift went full-send into that album's look, feel, costume, [color blocking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Color_blocking "Color blocking"), and more. [...] *The Eras Tour* is a feat. It’s live music at its highest spectacle and greatest excess. And for most, without the catalog and showmanship of Swift, it’d be too much. But 17 years into her career, maybe we ought to stop being surprised when she finds a way to top her own efforts year after year.

— David Waiss Aramesh, "Taylor Swift's The Eras Tour Is a 3-Hour Career-Spanning Victory Lap", *[Rolling Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone "Rolling Stone")*[[113]](#cite_note-Aramesh-2023-115)

The tour received "overwhelmingly positive" reviews from music and entertainment critics,[[157]](#cite_note-Kennedy-2023-160)[[158]](#cite_note-161) who praised both the high-end spectacle and "polished artistry" of the tour.[[159]](#cite_note-Sisario-2023-162) *[Billboard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_(magazine) "Billboard (magazine)")* described the Eras Tour as "the must-see blockbuster of the year".[[160]](#cite_note-163) [Neil McCormick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neil_McCormick "Neil McCormick") of *[The Daily Telegraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Daily_Telegraph "The Daily Telegraph"),* Keiran Southern of *[The Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times "The Times"),* Adrian Horton of *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian "The Guardian")*, Kelsey Barnes of *[The Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Independent "The Independent"),* Ilana Kaplan of the *[i](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_(newspaper) "I (newspaper)"),* and Erica Campbell of *[NME](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NME "NME")* gave the Eras Tour total five-star ratings. McCormick called the show "one of the most ambitious, spectacular, and charming stadium pop shows ever seen", lauding Swift's musicianship, vocals, and energy.[[106]](#cite_note-McCormick-2023-108) Southern declared the Eras Tour "a pop genius at the top of her game".[[161]](#cite_note-164) Horton praised the "rapturous" music selection, concept, "extravagant" staging, and Swift's stamina and vocals.[[111]](#cite_note-Horton-2023-113) Barnes noted the tour as "a career-defining spectacle" with acts marking the shifts in Swift's artistry,[[116]](#cite_note-Barnes-2023-118) while Kaplan commended the "unparalleled" showmanship, "spicier" choreography, [camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_(style) "Camp (style)") styles, and "seamless" transitions between acts.[[162]](#cite_note-Kaplan-2023-165) Campbell praised the storytelling aspect of the show that ties all the 10 acts together, enhanced by staging, cinematic ambience and fashion.[[163]](#cite_note-Campbell-2023-166) *[Australian Financial Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Financial_Review "Australian Financial Review")*'s James Thomson opined, "Swift works the massive stage like a master, cycling through countless costume changes and several elaborate sets, all while belting out 44 songs over three-and-a-half hours."[[164]](#cite_note-167)

[![Taylor Swift performs on stage with three purple clouds around her](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Midnights_Era_Set_%2853110073118%29.jpg/120px-Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Midnights_Era_Set_%2853110073118%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Midnights_Era_Set_(53110073118).jpg)

[![Taylor Swift in a sparkling purple dress performing a chair dance choreography onstage](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Midnights_Era_Set_%2853109777579%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/120px-Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Midnights_Era_Set_%2853109777579%29_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Midnights_Era_Set_(53109777579)_(cropped).jpg)

The show's performance styles, fashion and transitions were subjects of critical praise.[[162]](#cite_note-Kaplan-2023-165)[[163]](#cite_note-Campbell-2023-166) Pictured is Swift's costume change from a glittery oversized shirt to a rhinestone-adorned bodysuit while singing "[Midnight Rain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midnight_Rain "Midnight Rain")".

The versatility of the show's music, visuals, and performance art was often a point of praise in its reviews. Journalists Rebecca Lewis and Carson Mlnarik of *[Hello!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hello!_(magazine) "Hello! (magazine)")* and [MTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTV "MTV"), respectively, commended Swift's stage presence and commitment to her artistry; Lewis described Swift's [alter egos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alter_ego "Alter ego") during the tour as shifting from "country [ingénue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ingénue "Ingénue") to pop princess and folklore witch",[[139]](#cite_note-Lewis-2023-141) whereas Mlnarik affirmed that the on-screen visuals stayed true to every album's aesthetic.[[165]](#cite_note-168) *[The Week](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Week "The Week")* and *[Dallas Observer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas_Observer "Dallas Observer")* critics agreed, highlighting the "jaw-dropping" visuals and "bedazzled" fashion.[[157]](#cite_note-Kennedy-2023-160)[[105]](#cite_note-Raggio-107) *Billboard* editor Jason Lipshutz underscored Swift's "powerhouse" vocals, engaging artistic personas, and skill set.[[107]](#cite_note-Lipshutz-2023-109) [Jon Caramanica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Caramanica "Jon Caramanica") of *[The New York Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_York_Times "The New York Times")* highlighted the tour's scale, ambition, and portrayal of all the musical pivots of Swift's career,[[166]](#cite_note-169) whereas *[The Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Atlantic "The Atlantic")*'s Spencer Kornhaber complimented the show's art direction, suspense, and the sequencing of the acts.[[167]](#cite_note-170) Mikael Wood of *[Los Angeles Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Times "Los Angeles Times")* described the show as a "masterclass in pop ambition", showcasing Swift's range.[[140]](#cite_note-Wood-2023-142) According to *[The New Yorker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Yorker "The New Yorker")* senior editor Tyler Foggatt, the Eras Tour is a product of Swift's [understanding of herself](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-awareness "Self-awareness"), building a tour "solely devoted to the idea of a trajectory—that of a career, of a musical identity, of a life—that can be traced cleanly from one *era* to the next."[[168]](#cite_note-171)

Critics also appreciated the tour's production value and artistic direction.
