Philip Cosores of *[Uproxx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uproxx "Uproxx")* dubbed it the "most impressive stadium show ever conceived".[[130]](#cite_note-Cosores-2023-132) *[Spin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spin_(magazine) "Spin (magazine)")* critic Jonathan Cohen admired the rich stage design, usage of "state-of-the-art" technology, and immersive experience into Swift's "increasingly accomplished musical world-building".[[108]](#cite_note-Cohen-2023-110) *[Variety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variety_(magazine) "Variety (magazine)")* journalist Chris Willman felt that the "epic" show demonstrated that "the person who has come up with the single greatest body of pop songwriting in the 21st century is also its most popular performer".[[110]](#cite_note-Willman-2023c-112) *[Pollstar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pollstar "Pollstar")*'s Christina Fuoco and Andy Gensler praised the production as "stunning and tastefully rendered" and "live music at its highest spectacle".[[7]](#cite_note-Pollstar-2023c-9)[[104]](#cite_note-Pollstar-2023a-106) *Rolling Stone*'s Waiss David Aramesh opined that it is "a production spectacle of the highest echelon".[[113]](#cite_note-Aramesh-2023-115) Melinda Sheckels of *[Consequence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consequence_(publication) "Consequence (publication)")* praised the tour's "nuanced and interpretive" approach in depicting Swift's albums and the "sheer magnitude, artistry, and technical prowess" of the production.[[109]](#cite_note-Young-2023-111)

The revamped tour, following the release of *The Tortured Poets Department*, continued to receive rave reviews. Annabel Nugent of *The Independent* and Fiona Sheperd of *[The Scotsman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Scotsman "The Scotsman")* praised the tour's choreography, state-of-the-art production, song selection, and Swift's consistent energy.[[169]](#cite_note-172)[[170]](#cite_note-173) McCormick declared, "there's no doubt that Taylor Swift is the best in the world right now."[[171]](#cite_note-174) Critics Mark Sutherland and Anna Leszkiewicz opined that the tour's scale and performances continued to impress despite crossing 100 shows and being available as a film on streaming; Leszkiewicz, in *[New Statesman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Statesman "New Statesman")*, described Swift as "a talented actress" with a "high [camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_(style) "Camp (style)")" stage presence—"whether flashing faux-coy smiles, luxuriating in overdramatic eyerolls, or throwing herself into theatrical [Wicked Witch of the West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wicked_Witch_of_the_West "Wicked Witch of the West") arm movements."[[172]](#cite_note-175)[[173]](#cite_note-176) Petridis described the tour in *The Guardian* as "an astonishing, risk-taking, strangely intimate extravaganza", adding it is pointless to review the tour anymore as "every conceivable detail has already been dissected and discussed in depth."[[119]](#cite_note-Petridis-2024-121) Reviews published in the *[Berliner Zeitung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berliner_Zeitung "Berliner Zeitung")* and *[Süddeutsche Zeitung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Süddeutsche_Zeitung "Süddeutsche Zeitung")* praised Swift for what they considered as a commanding stage presence and charisma that is rare in her peers.[[174]](#cite_note-177)[[175]](#cite_note-178)

## Commercial performance

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=14 "Edit section: Commercial performance")]

### Box score

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=15 "Edit section: Box score")]

The Eras Tour broke a string of ticket sales records worldwide. In the first day of the US presale alone, the tour sold over 2.4 million tickets, the most sold by an artist in a single day,[[176]](#cite_note-179) surpassing [Robbie Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robbie_Williams "Robbie Williams"), who had sold 1.6 million tickets for his [Close Encounters Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Close_Encounters_Tour "Close Encounters Tour") in 2005.[[177]](#cite_note-180) *Billboard* reported on December 15 that the Eras Tour had already grossed an estimated US$554 million, and projected the US leg to finish with $591 million, surpassing the former all-time female record set by [Madonna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madonna "Madonna")'s [Sticky & Sweet Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sticky_&_Sweet_Tour "Sticky & Sweet Tour") ($407 million) in 2008–2009.[[178]](#cite_note-Brooks-2022-181)[[179]](#cite_note-182) Following the tour's launch, Swift rose to number one on *Pollstar*'s Artist Power Index chart.[[180]](#cite_note-183) [MetLife Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MetLife_Stadium "MetLife Stadium") named her their "No. 1 best-selling artist" of all time after the tour's third East Rutherford show, which was the 100th concert in the stadium's history.[[181]](#cite_note-MetLife_Stadium-2023-184)

In June 2023, *The Wall Street Journal* reported that the Eras Tour "on track to become the biggest in concert history, with the potential to gross over $1 billion";[[102]](#cite_note-Shah-2023-104) *Pollstar* estimates projected a $1.4 billion gross.[[182]](#cite_note-Pollstar-2023b-185) [Bloomberg News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomberg_News "Bloomberg News") reported that the average gross per show on the US leg was $13 million.[[183]](#cite_note-186) According to *[Forbes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forbes "Forbes")*, the Eras Tour has grossed $780 million from 56 shows as of August 2023, making it the [highest-grossing tour by a woman in history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_concert_tours_by_women "List of highest-grossing concert tours by women") based on its first North American leg alone, surpassing [Beyoncé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beyoncé "Beyoncé")'s [Renaissance World Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance_World_Tour "Renaissance World Tour").[[184]](#cite_note-187) In November 2023, [*Billboard*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billboard_(magazine) "Billboard (magazine)") estimated a gross of over $900 million from the tour thus far and that the total could nearly double after the 2024 shows.[[185]](#cite_note-188) Swift's team stated they do not intend to announce the box score,[[186]](#cite_note-189) but *Pollstar* reported in December 2023 an estimated gross of $1,039,263,762 from 60 shows that year, making the Eras Tour the [highest-grossing concert tour ever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_highest-grossing_concert_tours "List of highest-grossing concert tours") and the first to reach the $1 billion mark.[[101]](#cite_note-Gensler-2023-103) In June 2024, the [BBC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_News "BBC News") estimated 11 million tickets sold across the entire tour.[[187]](#cite_note-190) In October 2024, *Forbes* reported that the tour had grossed an additional $1.15 billion from 65 shows in 2024, bringing the tour's estimated gross up to $1.93 billion.[[188]](#cite_note-191)

After the tour concluded in December 2024, *The New York Times* published the first gross reported by Taylor Swift Touring, Swift's production company. The publication revealed that the tour grossed $2.07 billion with an attendance of 10.1 million.[[1]](#cite_note-Sisario-2024-3) *Pollstar*'s final estimate was $2.2 billion from 10.055 million tickets, making Swift the first solo act in history to sell over 10 million tickets on a single tour.[[189]](#cite_note-Pollstar-2024a-192) With an average of 67,487 tickets, she broke [U2 360° Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U2_360°_Tour "U2 360° Tour")'s record for highest attendance per show.[[189]](#cite_note-Pollstar-2024a-192) [*Inc.*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inc._(magazine) "Inc. (magazine)") opined that Swift could have made an additional $1.5 billion if she had adopted traditional ticketing strategies like other artists.[[190]](#cite_note-193)

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=16 "Edit section: Venue records")]

## Spin-off media

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=17 "Edit section: Spin-off media")]

Further information: [Taylor Swift: The Eras Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift:_The_Eras_Tour "Taylor Swift: The Eras Tour"), [The Eras Tour Book](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Eras_Tour_Book "The Eras Tour Book"), and [The End of an Era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift:_The_End_of_an_Era "Taylor Swift: The End of an Era")

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Speak_Now_Set_Era_%2853109969638%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/120px-Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Speak_Now_Set_Era_%2853109969638%29_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_Speak_Now_Set_Era_(53109969638)_(cropped).jpg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/Taylor_Swift_Eras_08092023-581_%281%29.jpg/120px-Taylor_Swift_Eras_08092023-581_%281%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_Eras_08092023-581_(1).jpg)

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_1989_Era_Set_%2853110043448%29_%28cropped%29.jpg/120px-Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_1989_Era_Set_%2853110043448%29_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taylor_Swift_The_Eras_Tour_1989_Era_Set_(53110043448)_(cropped).jpg)

Swift wore blue variants of her outfits for the *[Speak Now](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speak_Now "Speak Now")*, *[Folklore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore_(Taylor_Swift_album) "Folklore (Taylor Swift album)")*, and *[1989](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_(Taylor_Swift_album) "1989 (Taylor Swift album)")* acts to tease *[1989 (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_(Taylor's_Version) "1989 (Taylor's Version)")*, her fourth re-recorded album, at the final show in Los Angeles's [SoFi Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SoFi_Stadium "SoFi Stadium") on August 9, 2023.[[233]](#cite_note-Norwin-2023-237)

Swift unveiled various musical works during the tour. On the day of the opening show, she released four songs to celebrate the tour's launch: the re-recordings of "[Eyes Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eyes_Open_(Taylor's_Version) "Eyes Open (Taylor's Version)")" and "[Safe & Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safe_&_Sound_(Taylor's_Version) "Safe & Sound (Taylor's Version)")", both originally from the 2012 soundtrack *[The Hunger Games: Songs from District 12 and Beyond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hunger_Games:_Songs_from_District_12_and_Beyond "The Hunger Games: Songs from District 12 and Beyond")*; a re-recording of "[If This Was a Movie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/If_This_Was_a_Movie "If This Was a Movie")", one of the deluxe tracks from *[Speak Now](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speak_Now "Speak Now")* (2010); and "[All of the Girls You Loved Before](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_of_the_Girls_You_Loved_Before "All of the Girls You Loved Before")", a previously unreleased outtake of *Lover*.[[234]](#cite_note-238)

A special CD edition of *Midnights*, subtitled *The Late Night Edition*, was released during the Eras Tour. It was only purchasable in-person at merchandise stands at certain shows, beginning in [East Rutherford, New Jersey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Rutherford,_New_Jersey "East Rutherford, New Jersey"), on May 26, 2023. It exclusively contained the bonus track "[You're Losing Me](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/You're_Losing_Me "You're Losing Me")", which became a "ravenously anticipated" song; *Variety* reported that "fans were so eager to get their hands" on the CD, causing queues at the stadium the day before the merchandise store opened.[[235]](#cite_note-239)[[236]](#cite_note-240)[[237]](#cite_note-241)

On May 5, 2023, at the first [Nashville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashville "Nashville") show of the tour, Swift announced her third [re-recorded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Re-recorded "Re-recorded") album, *[Speak Now (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speak_Now_(Taylor's_Version) "Speak Now (Taylor's Version)"),* and its release date as July 7.[[238]](#cite_note-242) A digital deluxe version of *Speak Now (Taylor's Version)* contained two live recordings from the tour as bonus tracks.[[239]](#cite_note-243) She also premiered two [music videos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_videos "Music videos"), both of which she wrote and directed, during the tour. She premiered the videos for "Karma" featuring American rapper [Ice Spice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ice_Spice "Ice Spice") and "[I Can See You](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Can_See_You_(song) "I Can See You (song)")" before the acoustic set at the first East Rutherford show and first Kansas City show, respectively.[[240]](#cite_note-244)[[241]](#cite_note-245) On August 9, at the final [Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles "Los Angeles") show, Swift announced *[1989 (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_(Taylor's_Version) "1989 (Taylor's Version)")* as her fourth re-recorded album, which was released on October 27, exactly nine years after the release of the original *1989* album.[[233]](#cite_note-Norwin-2023-237) On November 3, [*Hits*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hits_(magazine) "Hits (magazine)") reported that a [live album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Live_album "Live album") of the tour was discussed.[[242]](#cite_note-246)

On October 13, 2023, Swift released the self-produced [concert film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concert_film "Concert film"), *Taylor Swift: The Eras Tour*, directed by Sam Wrench, to theaters worldwide. It was produced from the footage recorded at the tour's Los Angeles shows.[[243]](#cite_note-247)[[244]](#cite_note-248) The film, in an unprecedented move, had Swift partnering directly with the theaters to both [distribute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_distribution "Film distribution") and [exhibit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_exhibitor "Film exhibitor") the film instead of a [major film studio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_film_studio "Major film studio").[[245]](#cite_note-249)[[246]](#cite_note-Moreau-2023-250) It received critical acclaim for capturing the spectacle and energy of the show and became [the highest-grossing concert film of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concert_film#Highest-grossing_concert_films "Concert film").[[246]](#cite_note-Moreau-2023-250)[[247]](#cite_note-251)

On February 16, 2024, at the first [Melbourne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melbourne "Melbourne") show, Swift announced a second variant of *The Tortured Poets Department*, with a bonus track titled "The Bolter".[[248]](#cite_note-252) She announced another variant of the album, with the bonus track "The Albatross" on February 23, at the first [Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney "Sydney") show.[[249]](#cite_note-253) The fourth variant of the album, containing the bonus track "[The Black Dog](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Black_Dog_(song) "The Black Dog (song)")", was announced on March 3, at the second [Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore "Singapore") show of the tour.[[250]](#cite_note-254) On August 20, Swift premiered the music video for "[I Can Do It with a Broken Heart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Can_Do_It_with_a_Broken_Heart "I Can Do It with a Broken Heart")", a song inspired by the tour after leaving the stage at the end of the eighth show in London. The video features footage of tour performances, rehearsals, audiences, and backstage moments.[[251]](#cite_note-255)[[252]](#cite_note-256) Throughout the tour Swift released limited-time digital deluxe versions of *The Tortured Poets Department*, each with a different live recording of songs from the album.[[253]](#cite_note-257)[[254]](#cite_note-258)[[255]](#cite_note-259)[[256]](#cite_note-260)

On October 15, 2024, Swift announced her first-ever book, *The Eras Tour Book.*[[257]](#cite_note-261) Dubbed "the official retrospective" of the tour,[[258]](#cite_note-262) it is a 256-page [photo book](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Photo_book "Photo book") with over 500 on-stage and behind-the-scenes images, as well as Swift's personal reflections and notes.[[259]](#cite_note-263)[[260]](#cite_note-264) Concurrently, Swift launched her in-house publishing imprint, Taylor Swift Publications.[[261]](#cite_note-265)[[262]](#cite_note-266)[[263]](#cite_note-267) The book was released exclusively in [Target](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Target_Corporation "Target Corporation") stores on a [Black Friday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Friday_(shopping) "Black Friday (shopping)")—November 29, 2024.[[264]](#cite_note-268) Selling over a million copies in its first week in the US alone, the book became the best-selling book of 2024.[[265]](#cite_note-269) On January 16, 2025, Swift released three limited-time digital versions of *[Lover (Live from Paris)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lover_(Live_from_Paris) "Lover (Live from Paris)")*, each with a live recording of a song from *Lover* performed on the tour.[[266]](#cite_note-270)

A documentary series, *The End of an Era*, premiered through [Disney+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney+ "Disney+") on December 12, 2025, released over the course of a three-week period, concluding on December 23.[[267]](#cite_note-Beaumont-Thomas-2025-271) Directed by [Don Argott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Don_Argott "Don Argott") and produced by Object & Animal, the six-episode series features an "intimate look" at Swift's life while on tour.[[268]](#cite_note-Willman-2025-272)[[269]](#cite_note-273) Additionally, a second concert film documenting the tour was released on December 12. Directed by [Glenn Weiss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenn_Weiss "Glenn Weiss") and filmed at the tour's final show in Vancouver in December 2024, *The Eras Tour: The Final Show* includes *The Tortured Poets Department* act, which was absent from the first concert film.[[267]](#cite_note-Beaumont-Thomas-2025-271)[[268]](#cite_note-Willman-2025-272)

## Impact

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=19 "Edit section: Impact")]

Main article: [Impact of the Eras Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impact_of_the_Eras_Tour "Impact of the Eras Tour")

> Taylor Swift keeps building the legend of her Eras Tour, week after week, city by city, making every night so much longer, wilder, louder, more jubilant than it has to be. There's nothing in history to compare. This is her best tour ever, by an absurd margin. It's a journey through her past, starring all the different Taylors she's ever been, which means all the Taylors that you've ever been.

— [Rob Sheffield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rob_Sheffield "Rob Sheffield"), *[Rolling Stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rolling_Stone "Rolling Stone")*[[286]](#cite_note-290)

The Eras Tour had a significant impact on the [music industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_industry "Music industry"), [entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entertainment "Entertainment") and beyond. It was described as one of the most prominent cultural phenomena of the 21st century,[[159]](#cite_note-Sisario-2023-162)[[287]](#cite_note-291)[[288]](#cite_note-Petrusich-2023-292) generating a level of attention similar to the 1960s' [Beatlemania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beatlemania "Beatlemania").[[289]](#cite_note-293) The tour revitalized local businesses and [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism "Tourism"),[[290]](#cite_note-294)[[291]](#cite_note-295)[[182]](#cite_note-Pollstar-2023b-185) attracted large crowds of spectators outside stadiums,[[292]](#cite_note-296)[[293]](#cite_note-297) dominated [news cycles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News_cycle "News cycle") and [social media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_media "Social media"),[[294]](#cite_note-298)[[295]](#cite_note-299) and inspired tributes from [governments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government "Government") and organizations.[[296]](#cite_note-300) Critics often described the Eras Tour as a monocultural event demonstrating [Swift's impact on popular culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_impact_of_Taylor_Swift "Cultural impact of Taylor Swift").[[288]](#cite_note-Petrusich-2023-292) The tour fueled an increase in public consumption of her discography as well.[[297]](#cite_note-301) Swift's [net worth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_worth "Net worth"), which was $740 million before the tour began, was updated to $1.1 billion after the first 57 shows of the tour, making Swift the first [billionaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billionaire "Billionaire") in history with music as the main source of income.[[298]](#cite_note-302)[[299]](#cite_note-303) *[Sportico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sportico "Sportico")* reported that the NFL stadiums Swift performed at garnered substantial revenue from concessions and merchandise sales, and parking permits.[[300]](#cite_note-304) A carbon footprint analysis by the climate consultancy Greenly estimated the tour's emissions, noting that merchandise was a notable contributor. By its calculations, if 25,000 attendees at each show bought a single T-shirt, the resulting emissions would amount to roughly 19,370 tonnes of CO2.[[301]](#cite_note-305)[[302]](#cite_note-306)

## Controversies

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=20 "Edit section: Controversies")]

### US ticket sales crash

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=21 "Edit section: US ticket sales crash")]

Main article: [Taylor Swift–Ticketmaster controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift–Ticketmaster_controversy "Taylor Swift–Ticketmaster controversy")

[![Federal Trade Commission Building in 2007](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Federal_Trade_Commission_DC_2007.jpg/250px-Federal_Trade_Commission_DC_2007.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Federal_Trade_Commission_DC_2007.jpg)

The US [Federal Trade Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Trade_Commission "Federal Trade Commission") proposed to outlaw [junk fees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junk_fees "Junk fees") in the country following the controversy.[[303]](#cite_note-307)

On November 15, the day of the first US presale, Ticketmaster's website [crashed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crash_(computing) "Crash (computing)") following what *Variety* called "historically unprecedented demand".[[304]](#cite_note-308) [Live Nation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Live_Nation "Live Nation"), the parent company of Ticketmaster, said they prepared for 1.5 million verified fans, but 14 million turned up;[[305]](#cite_note-309) published a statement saying they had been "unprepared" to accommodate them;[[5]](#cite_note-Jones-2022-7) and cancelled any further sales, citing inability to meet demand.[[306]](#cite_note-310) The fiasco was the subject of public criticism and political scrutiny. Customers and fans criticized Ticketmaster for its allegedly flawed systems and inefficient technical resolutions.[[307]](#cite_note-311)[[308]](#cite_note-312) US lawmakers, including [attorneys general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorneys_general "Attorneys general") and members of [Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Congress "United States Congress"), took notice of the issue,[[309]](#cite_note-313) which became the focus of multiple state, federal and congressional inquiries,[[178]](#cite_note-Brooks-2022-181)[[310]](#cite_note-314) and [an antitrust lawsuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_v._Live_Nation_Entertainment "United States v. Live Nation Entertainment").[[311]](#cite_note-315)

### Venue management in Brazil

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=22 "Edit section: Venue management in Brazil")]

Main article: [Death of Ana Clara Benevides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Ana_Clara_Benevides "Death of Ana Clara Benevides")

[![Estádio Olímpico João Havelange](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Est%C3%A1dio_Ol%C3%ADmpico_Jo%C3%A3o_Havelange_-_Rio_de_Janeiro%2C_Brasil_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Est%C3%A1dio_Ol%C3%ADmpico_Jo%C3%A3o_Havelange_-_Rio_de_Janeiro%2C_Brasil_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Estádio_Olímpico_João_Havelange_-_Rio_de_Janeiro,_Brasil_(cropped).jpg)

[Estádio Olímpico Nilton Santos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estádio_Olímpico_Nilton_Santos "Estádio Olímpico Nilton Santos"), where the incident took place[[312]](#cite_note-316)

Unrest was reported on June 11, 2023, outside the Brazilian venues' box offices, as scalpers attempted to [cut in line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cut_in_line "Cut in line"), including some armed scalpers who threatened violence, until the police intervened.[[313]](#cite_note-317) The agency responsible for the tour in Brazil, [T4F – Time For Fun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T4F_–_Time_For_Fun "T4F – Time For Fun") (T4F), was reported over 100 times to authorities for their inaction.[[314]](#cite_note-318) On June 20, [federal lawmakers in Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Congress_of_Brazil "National Congress of Brazil") filed the "Taylor Swift Law", which would penalize scalpers with up to four years in prison and a fine of up to 100 times the value of the ticket.[[315]](#cite_note-319)

On November 17, 2023, before the start of the first Rio de Janeiro show, T4F reportedly prohibited concertgoers from bringing their own water bottles inside [the venue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estádio_Olímpico_Nilton_Santos "Estádio Olímpico Nilton Santos"). It was one of the hottest days in the city, with a [heat index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heat_index "Heat index") of 59.3 °C (138.74 °F),[[316]](#cite_note-Wu-2023-320) as part of [a heat wave in the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2023_Brazil_heat_wave "November 2023 Brazil heat wave"). Videos posted to social media by fans showed thousands of queued concertgoers waiting for hours in the sun before entry into the stadium;[[317]](#cite_note-Jeantet-2023-321) Swift and her team arranged and distributed water bottles to the crowd.[[318]](#cite_note-Losciale-2023-322) A concert attendee, Ana Clara Benevides, died while being taken to a hospital after she fainted during the first few minutes of the show.[[316]](#cite_note-Wu-2023-320) T4F attracted widespread criticism from fans and politicians.[[317]](#cite_note-Jeantet-2023-321) Several claimed that stadium organizers "refused" to provide concertgoers water; a criminal investigation was opened.[[319]](#cite_note-Ayres-2023-323)[[318]](#cite_note-Losciale-2023-322) Benevides' cause of death was forensically determined to be [cardiac arrest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiac_arrest "Cardiac arrest") caused by [heat exhaustion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heat_exhaustion "Heat exhaustion").[[320]](#cite_note-324) Swift grieved Benevides' death on social media.[[319]](#cite_note-Ayres-2023-323) She postponed the show that had been scheduled for November 18 to 20, citing "extreme temperatures".[[321]](#cite_note-Sachdeva-2023-325)

### Diplomacy in Asia

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=23 "Edit section: Diplomacy in Asia")]

[Srettha Thavisin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srettha_Thavisin "Srettha Thavisin"), then [Prime Minister of Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Thailand "Prime Minister of Thailand"), claimed that the [government of Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Singapore "Government of Singapore") offered subsidies of $2 million–$3 million per show of the Eras Tour in exchange for making Singapore the exclusive venue in [Southeast Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Asia "Southeast Asia").[[322]](#cite_note-326) He criticized Singapore for excluding other member countries of the [ASEAN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASEAN "ASEAN") from witnessing Swift.[[323]](#cite_note-327) The [Singapore Tourism Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore_Tourism_Board "Singapore Tourism Board") responded that they did provide a "grant" to bring the Eras Tour to Singapore, and that the [Ministry of Culture, Community and Youth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Culture,_Community_and_Youth "Ministry of Culture, Community and Youth") worked with the tour's promoter, [Anschutz Entertainment Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anschutz_Entertainment_Group "Anschutz Entertainment Group") (AEG), but did not reveal the size of the grant or attached conditions due to "business confidentiality".[[324]](#cite_note-328) Minister [Edwin Tong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edwin_Tong "Edwin Tong") commented, the alleged sum of the grant was "nowhere as high as what is being speculated".[[325]](#cite_note-329) [Joey Salceda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joey_Salceda "Joey Salceda"), member of the [House of Representatives of the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Representatives_of_the_Philippines "House of Representatives of the Philippines"), criticized Singapore and demanded that the Philippine [Department of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Foreign_Affairs_(Philippines) "Department of Foreign Affairs (Philippines)") seek an explanation from the [Embassy of Singapore in Manila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_Singapore,_Manila "Embassy of Singapore, Manila").[[326]](#cite_note-330)[[327]](#cite_note-331) [Sandiaga Uno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandiaga_Uno "Sandiaga Uno"), Indonesian [minister of Tourism and Creative Economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Tourism_and_Creative_Economy "Ministry of Tourism and Creative Economy"), also expressed his disappointment, stating "Indonesia was eager to replicate the success of [Swiftonomics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impact_of_the_Eras_Tour#Economy_and_commerce "Impact of the Eras Tour")".[[328]](#cite_note-332) Subsequently, [Lee Hsien Loong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lee_Hsien_Loong "Lee Hsien Loong"), [Prime Minister of Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Singapore "Prime Minister of Singapore") since 2004, assured that the exclusivity grant did not have an hostile intention.[[329]](#cite_note-333)

Swift included neither the [People's Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Republic_of_China "People's Republic of China") nor the [Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China "Republic of China") (Taiwan) in the Eras Tour.[[330]](#cite_note-The_Independent-2024-334) The tour skipping Taiwan was a topic of political debate during [the campaigns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_campaign "Political campaign") preceding the [2024 Taiwanese presidential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Taiwanese_presidential_election "2024 Taiwanese presidential election") and [legislative body elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Taiwanese_legislative_election "2024 Taiwanese legislative election"). [Jaw Shaw-kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaw_Shaw-kong "Jaw Shaw-kong"), vice-presidential candidate of the opposition ([Kuomintang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuomintang "Kuomintang") party), claimed he invited Swift to perform at the [Taipei Dome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taipei_Dome "Taipei Dome") and that she initially agreed to perform but later declined due to "geopolitical risks" of the sensitive [cross-strait relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cross-strait_relations "Cross-strait relations"). The [Taiwanese Ministry of Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Culture_(Taiwan) "Ministry of Culture (Taiwan)") commented on the issue but neither denied nor confirmed Jaw's claims. [Kaohsiung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung "Kaohsiung") mayor [Chen Chi-mai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chen_Chi-mai "Chen Chi-mai") described Jaw's claims about Swift as an attempt at manipulating the voters.[[330]](#cite_note-The_Independent-2024-334)[[331]](#cite_note-335)[[332]](#cite_note-336)

### Security in Europe

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=24 "Edit section: Security in Europe")]

Further information: [2024 Southport stabbing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Southport_stabbing "2024 Southport stabbing"), [2024 Vienna terrorism plot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Vienna_terrorism_plot "2024 Vienna terrorism plot"), and [2024 Labour Party freebies controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Labour_Party_freebies_controversy "2024 Labour Party freebies controversy")

[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Southport_riot.jpg/250px-Southport_riot.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Southport_riot.jpg)

A [police vehicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Police_transport "Police transport") set afire by rioters following [a fatal stabbing attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Southport_stabbings "2024 Southport stabbings") on children in a [Swiftie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swiftie "Swiftie") workshop in [Southport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southport "Southport"), United Kingdom

On July 18, 2024, a suspected stalker of Swift, who was accused of threatening her and her boyfriend, American football player [Travis Kelce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travis_Kelce "Travis Kelce"), on social media was detained and arrested as he attempted to enter the second of Swift's shows in [Gelsenkirchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gelsenkirchen "Gelsenkirchen").[[333]](#cite_note-337) The same month, three children were killed at a [mass stabbing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_stabbing "Mass stabbing") attack at a [Swiftie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swiftie "Swiftie") workshop in [Southport, Merseyside](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southport,_Merseyside "Southport, Merseyside"), UK. The incident caused public unrest in Southport, and over the next few days, escalated into [nationwide protests and riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_Kingdom_riots "2024 United Kingdom riots").[[334]](#cite_note-338)[[335]](#cite_note-Reid-2024-339) A week later, authorities thwarted an [Islamic State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_State "Islamic State") (ISIS) plot to attack Swift's three shows in [Vienna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vienna "Vienna").[[336]](#cite_note-340) Police arrested three teenagers who were "radicalized on the Internet" and plotted to kill "tens of thousands" at the concerts.[[337]](#cite_note-341) Initially, Austrian public security director Franz Ruf stated that the shows would take place as scheduled, with additional security measures in place.[[338]](#cite_note-342)[[339]](#cite_note-Franz-2024-343)[[340]](#cite_note-344)[[341]](#cite_note-345) However, event organizer Barracuda Music announced that all three shows would be cancelled with tickets refunded, after confirmation from the [Government of Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Austria "Government of Austria") of an elaborate terrorist plan.[[342]](#cite_note-346) With Swift's permission, the Austrian public broadcaster [ORF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ORF_(broadcaster) "ORF (broadcaster)") aired the tour's [accompanying concert film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift:_The_Eras_Tour "Taylor Swift: The Eras Tour") on August 10 on [ORF 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ORF_1 "ORF 1") television for free.[[343]](#cite_note-347) The tour's next stop, London, increased its security for the shows.[[344]](#cite_note-348)

In October 2024, controversy ensued in the UK when it was reported that politicians of the ruling [Labour Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labour_Party_(UK) "Labour Party (UK)") were gifted with over £20,000 in free tickets to the Eras Tour after Swift's demands for the highest level of security were denied, with the politicians allegedly pressuring the [Metropolitan Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Police "Metropolitan Police") to give in to the demands.[[345]](#cite_note-Buchanan-2024-349) In advance of the London dates, Swift's team had reportedly demanded additional "VVIP" security from the Metropolitan Police's [Special Escort Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Escort_Group_(Metropolitan_Police) "Special Escort Group (Metropolitan Police)") and threatened to cancel the London shows if not provided.[[345]](#cite_note-Buchanan-2024-349) The police initially turned down the request, claiming their intelligence did not detect any threat to Swift's London shows and hence she did not require the top-level "taxpayer-funded" security reserved for [heads of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heads_of_state "Heads of state").[[345]](#cite_note-Buchanan-2024-349)[[346]](#cite_note-350) However, the police subsequently reversed its decision and provided Swift with the requested protection.[[345]](#cite_note-Buchanan-2024-349) Various politicians of the opposition [Conservative Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_(UK) "Conservative Party (UK)"), such as [Susan Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susan_Hall "Susan Hall"), [Robert Jenrick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Jenrick "Robert Jenrick"), [Gavin Williamson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gavin_Williamson "Gavin Williamson") and [Andrew Murrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Murrison "Andrew Murrison"), criticized the Labour government, accusing it of [abuse of power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abuse_of_power "Abuse of power").[[347]](#cite_note-351)[[348]](#cite_note-352)[[349]](#cite_note-353) Both the police and the government denied the allegations of [bribery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bribery "Bribery");[[335]](#cite_note-Reid-2024-339) Starmer's spokesperson described it as a [conflict of interest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conflict_of_interest "Conflict of interest").[[350]](#cite_note-354)

## Philanthropy

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=25 "Edit section: Philanthropy")]

Swift donated to [food bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_bank "Food bank") units at every stop of the Eras Tour, as reported by the respective organizations,[[351]](#cite_note-355)[[352]](#cite_note-356) and exclusively employed various local businesses to fulfill her crew's daily requirements.[[353]](#cite_note-357) At the conclusion of the first US leg, Swift gave "unprecedented" [bonus payments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bonus_payment "Bonus payment") totaling over $55 million to her entire touring crew,[[354]](#cite_note-358)[[355]](#cite_note-359) including $100,000 to each of the 50 truck drivers involved in transporting the stage setup and production equipment.[[356]](#cite_note-360) By the end of the tour, Swift distributed $197 million in bonus payments to her crew.[[357]](#cite_note-361) A spokesperson for Swift said she purchased more than double the [carbon credits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_credits "Carbon credits") needed to offset [emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenhouse_gas_emissions "Greenhouse gas emissions") generated by her travel throughout the tour.[[358]](#cite_note-362) In October 2023, Swift donated Eras Tour tickets to the Rare Impact Fund, a charity and mental health awareness initiative by the American singer [Selena Gomez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selena_Gomez "Selena Gomez")'s cosmetic company, [Rare Beauty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rare_Beauty "Rare Beauty"). The auctioned tickets sold for $15,000 and were the "biggest ticket item of the event".[[359]](#cite_note-363) On April 27, 2024, four Eras Tour tickets raised $80,000 at auction in a gala benefiting the 15 and The Mahomies Foundation, a charity by the American football player [Patrick Mahomes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Mahomes "Patrick Mahomes").[[360]](#cite_note-364)

#### Alterations

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=28 "Edit section: Alterations")]

* "[The 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_1 "The 1")" replaced "Invisible String" starting from the Arlington shows,[[362]](#cite_note-Gularte-2023-366) except at the second Nashville show, where Swift performed "Invisible String" in honor of the bench at [Centennial Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centennial_Park_(Nashville) "Centennial Park (Nashville)") dedicated to her.[[363]](#cite_note-367)
* "[Nothing New](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nothing_New_(song) "Nothing New (song)")" was added before "All Too Well (10 Minute Version)" at shows opened by [Phoebe Bridgers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoebe_Bridgers "Phoebe Bridgers"), who performed the song with Swift.[[364]](#cite_note-368)[[365]](#cite_note-369)
* At the three East Rutherford shows, Swift performed the remix of "Karma" with [Ice Spice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ice_Spice "Ice Spice").[[366]](#cite_note-370)
* "[Long Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Live_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Long Live (Taylor Swift song)")" was added after "Enchanted" as part of the *Speak Now* act after the release of *[Speak Now (Taylor's Version)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speak_Now_(Taylor's_Version) "Speak Now (Taylor's Version)")*, until May 2024.[[367]](#cite_note-371)[[362]](#cite_note-Gularte-2023-366)
* "[No Body, No Crime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_Body,_No_Crime "No Body, No Crime")" replaced "'Tis the Damn Season" at shows opened by [Haim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haim_(band) "Haim (band)"), who performed the song with Swift.[[368]](#cite_note-372)

#### Alterations

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=30 "Edit section: Alterations")]

* At the last London show and the three Miami shows, Swift performed "[Florida!!!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florida!!! "Florida!!!")" with [Florence Welch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florence_Welch "Florence Welch") of [Florence and the Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florence_and_the_Machine "Florence and the Machine") before "Who's Afraid of Little Old Me?"[[371]](#cite_note-375)[[372]](#cite_note-376)[[373]](#cite_note-377)

## Surprise songs

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=31 "Edit section: Surprise songs")]

Swift performed two numbers from her discography, as either a standalone song or a [mashup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mashup_(music) "Mashup (music)"), at each show as "surprise songs" in the ninth act—the first on acoustic guitar and the second on piano.[[147]](#cite_note-Iasimone-2024-149)[[374]](#cite_note-378) Over the course of the tour, Swift played every song in her discography, with the exception of "[That's When](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/That's_When "That's When")", "Bye Bye Baby", "Girl at Home", "[Ronan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronan_(song) "Ronan (song)")", "[Forever Winter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forever_Winter "Forever Winter")" and "[Soon You'll Get Better](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soon_You'll_Get_Better "Soon You'll Get Better")".[[375]](#cite_note-379)

| December 8 | "[A Place in This World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Place_in_This_World "A Place in This World")" / "[New Romantics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Romantics_(song) "New Romantics (song)")" | "[Long Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Live_(Taylor_Swift_song) "Long Live (Taylor Swift song)")" / "[New Year's Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Year's_Day_(Taylor_Swift_song) "New Year's Day (Taylor Swift song)")" / "The Manuscript" |  |

### Notes

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=34 "Edit section: Notes")]

* At the third Tampa show and the second Pittsburgh show, Swift reversed the order of the instruments she used to perform the surprise songs, playing the first on piano with [Aaron Dessner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_Dessner "Aaron Dessner") and the second on acoustic guitar.[[147]](#cite_note-Iasimone-2024-149)
* At the third Foxborough show, Swift performed both surprise songs on acoustic guitar due to technical difficulties with the piano caused by the previous night's rain.[[376]](#cite_note-381)
* At the second Cincinnati show, Swift performed "[I Miss You, I'm Sorry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_Miss_You,_I'm_Sorry "I Miss You, I'm Sorry")" on acoustic guitar with [Gracie Abrams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gracie_Abrams "Gracie Abrams") on piano between the first and second surprise songs.[[377]](#cite_note-382)

## Personnel

[[edit](/w/index.php?title=The_Eras_Tour&action=edit&section=37 "Edit section: Personnel")]

Adapted from the credits of *Taylor Swift: The Eras Tour* concert film.[[422]](#cite_note-447)[*[better source needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Questionable_sources "Wikipedia:Verifiability")*]

* [Taylor Swift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Swift "Taylor Swift") – lead vocals, guitar, piano

**Band & Vocalists**

* Mike Meadows – co-band leader, guitar, keyboards, cello, harmonica, mandolin, background vocals
* Max Bernstein – co-band leader, guitar, keyboards, pedal steel
* Paul Sidoti – guitar
* [Amos Heller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amos_Heller_(musician) "Amos Heller (musician)") – bass, keyboard bass
* Matthew Billingslea – drums
* Karina DePiano – keyboards
* Melanie Nyema – background vocalist section leader
* Kamilah Marshall – background vocalist dance captain

3. [&#8202;](#refnote-159)[↑](#ref-back-159-0) This is an [Easter egg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_egg_(media) "Easter egg (media)"), hinting at Swift's next studio album, *[The Life of a Showgirl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Life_of_a_Showgirl "The Life of a Showgirl")* (2025), which was conceived by Swift during the European leg of the tour.[[156]](#cite_note-158)
4. [&#8202;](#refnote-203)[↑](#ref-back-203-0) This record was broken by [Ed Sheeran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ed_Sheeran "Ed Sheeran")'s [+–=÷× Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/+–=÷×_Tour "+–=÷× Tour") concert on July 22, 2023.[[199]](#cite_note-202)
5. [&#8202;](#refnote-380)[↑](#ref-back-380-0) Due to rain the night before, Swift's piano was broken. As a result, she performed both surprise songs on the guitar.
6. [&#8202;](#refnote-390)[↑](#ref-back-390-0) Rough estimate based on the third night's record attendance.[[200]](#cite_note-Leimkuehler-2023-204)
7. [&#8202;](#refnote-392)[↑](#ref-back-392-0) The scheduled opening acts were Phoebe Bridgers and Gracie Abrams; however, due to delays caused by heavy [thunderstorms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thunderstorm "Thunderstorm"), both sets were cancelled.[[384]](#cite_note-391)
8. [&#8202;](#refnote-396)[↑](#ref-back-396-0) Rough estimate based on the first night's attendance.[[387]](#cite_note-395)
9. [&#8202;](#refnote-398)[↑](#ref-back-398-0) Rough estimate based on the second night's record attendance.[[205]](#cite_note-Acrisure_Stadium-2023-209)
10. [&#8202;](#refnote-402)[↑](#ref-back-402-0) The scheduled opening acts were Muna and Gracie Abrams; however, due to forecasts of heavy thunderstorms, Abrams' set was cancelled.[[391]](#cite_note-401)
11. [&#8202;](#refnote-405)[↑](#ref-back-405-0) Rough estimate based on the first night's record attendance.[[210]](#cite_note-The_Seattle_Times-2023-214)
12. [&#8202;](#refnote-413)[↑](#ref-back-413-0) The show on November 12, 2023, in Buenos Aires was originally scheduled for November 10, but was postponed due to heavy thunderstorms.[[399]](#cite_note-412)
13. [&#8202;](#refnote-415)[↑](#ref-back-415-0) Rough estimate based on the first night's record attendance.[[400]](#cite_note-414)
14. [&#8202;](#refnote-416)[↑](#ref-back-416-0) The show on November 20, 2023, in Rio de Janeiro was originally scheduled for November 18, but was postponed due to [extreme heat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2023_Brazil_heat_wave "November 2023 Brazil heat wave") and the [death of Ana Clara Benevides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Ana_Clara_Benevides "Death of Ana Clara Benevides").[[321]](#cite_note-Sachdeva-2023-325)
15. [&#8202;](#refnote-422)[↑](#ref-back-422-0) The scheduled opening act was Sabrina Carpenter; however, due to delays caused by thunderstorms, the set was cancelled, but she later came on during the surprise songs to perform with Taylor.[[405]](#cite_note-421)
16. [&#8202;](#refnote-424)[↑](#ref-back-424-0) Rough estimate based on the fourth night's record attendance.[[406]](#cite_note-423)
17. [&#8202;](#refnote-425)[↑](#ref-back-425-0) Rough estimate based on the sixth night's record attendance.[[218]](#cite_note-Sports_Hub_Singapore-2024-222)
18. [&#8202;](#refnote-429)[↑](#ref-back-429-0) Rough estimate based on the second night's record attendance.[[221]](#cite_note-West-2024-225)
19. [&#8202;](#refnote-431)[↑](#ref-back-431-0) Rough estimate based on the first night's record attendance.[[223]](#cite_note-SwiftLiverpool-227)
20. [&#8202;](#refnote-WEMBLEY)[1](#ref-back-WEMBLEY-0) [2](#ref-back-WEMBLEY-1) Attendance from the eight nights combined.[[1]](#cite_note-Sisario-2024-3)
21. [&#8202;](#refnote-441)[↑](#ref-back-441-0) Rough estimate based on the first night's attendance.[[417]](#cite_note-440)
